## P1 What happens when you type a URL into your browser_-(1080p)

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=1)　时长 5分21秒

**原文**

Hi, welcome to the first episode of bite-by-go system design video series. In these videos, we hope to share with you system design knowledge and tips in a different format. We are new to this, so please give us feedback in the comment section below. In this first video, we'll talk about HTTP. Here in this example, Bob enters a URL into the browser and hits Enter. What happens next? Well, let's first discuss what the URL is. URL stands for Universal Resource Locator, and it has four parts. First is Scheme. In this example is HTTP colon slash slash. This tells the browser to connect to the server using a protocol called HTTP. Another common scheme is HTTPS, with HTTPS the connection is encrypted. The second part of the URL is the domain. In this example, example.com, it is the domain name of the site. The third part of the URL is path, and the fourth is resource. The difference between these two is often not very clear. Just think of them as directory and file in a regular file system. They together specify the resource on the server we want to load. Okay, so Bob entered the URL into the browser. What happened next? Well, the browser needs to know how to reach the server. In this case example.com, this is done with a process called DNSLOOKUP. DNS stands for domain name system. Think of it as a phone book of the internet. DNS translates domain names to IP addresses, so browsers can no resources. It is an interesting service in an office self that deserves a dedicated video that we should make later. Now to make the lookup process fast, the DNS information is heavily cached. First, the browser itself caches it for a short period of time. And if it is not in the browser cache, the browser asks the operating system for it. The operating system itself has a cache for it which also keeps the answer for a short period of time. Now if the operating system doesn't have it, it makes a query out to the internet to a DNS resolver. This sets up a chain of requests until the IP addresses is resolved. Now this is a elaborate and elegant process, but just to keep in mind that this process involves many servers in the DNS infrastructure. And the answer is cached every step of the way. Again, we'll discuss this in detail in another video. Now finally, the browser has the IP address of the server, in our case again, example.com. Next, the browser establishes a TCP connection with the server using the IP address it got for it. Now there's a handshake involved in establishing a TCP connection. You take several network rank trips for this to complete. To keep the loading process fast, modern browsers use something called a keep-a-life connection to try to reuse an established TCP connection to the server as much as possible. One thing to note is that if the protocol is HTTP S, the process of establishing a new connection is even more involved. It requires a complicated process called SSL TLS handshake to establish a encrypted connection between the browser and the server. This handshake is expensive, and the browsers use tricks like SSL session resumption to try to lower the cost. Finally, the browser sends an HTTP request to the server over the established TCP connection. HTTP itself is a very simple protocol. The server processes the request and sends back a response. The browser receives the response and renders the HTML content. Oftentimes, there are additional resources to lower the JavaScript bundles and images. The browser repeats the process above that we mentioned, making DNS look up, establishing TCP connection, making HTTP requests to finish fetching all the other resources. If you'd like to learn more about system design, check out our books and weekly newsletter. And please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much, and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

欢迎来到 bite-by-go 系统设计视频系列的第一集。在这些视频中，我们希望通过一种不同的形式与您分享系统设计相关的知识和技巧。我们在这方面还是新手，因此请在下方的评论区给我们留言反馈。在本期首视频中，我们将讨论 HTTP。在这个例子中，Bob 在浏览器中输入一个 URL 并按下回车键。接下来会发生什么呢？首先，我们来谈谈什么是 URL。URL 是 Universal Resource Locator（统一资源定位符）的缩写，它由四个部分组成。第一部分是协议（Scheme）。在本例中是 HTTP 冒号双斜杠。这部分告诉浏览器使用名为 HTTP 的协议连接服务器。另一种常见的协议是 HTTPS，使用 HTTPS 时连接会被加密。URL 的第二部分是域名。在本例中是 example.com，这是该网站的域名。URL 的第三部分是路径（path），第四部分是资源（resource）。这两者之间的区别通常不太明确。您可以将它们想象成普通文件系统中的目录和文件。它们共同指定了我们希望从服务器加载的资源。好的，Bob 已经将 URL 输入到浏览器中，接下来发生了什么？浏览器需要知道如何访问该服务器。在本例中，example.com 是通过一个称为 DNSLOOKUP 的过程完成的。DNS 是域名系统（domain name system）的缩写。你可以把它想象成互联网的“电话簿”。DNS 将域名转换为 IP 地址，以便浏览器可以访问资源。这项服务在办公环境中非常有趣，值得我们之后专门制作一个视频来讲解。为了让查找过程更快，DNS 信息会被大量缓存。首先，浏览器本身会缓存这些信息一段时间。如果浏览器的缓存中没有，浏览器就会向操作系统请求。操作系统本身也有一个缓存，同样会保留答案一段时间。如果操作系统也没有，它就会向互联网上的一个 DNS 解析器发起查询。这样就建立了一个请求链，直到 IP 地址被解析出来。这是一个复杂而优雅的过程，但需要记住的是，这个过程涉及 DNS 基础设施中的许多服务器，并且答案在每一步都会被缓存。我们稍后会在另一个视频中详细讨论这一点。最后，浏览器就获得了服务器的 IP 地址——在本例中仍然是 example.com。接下来，浏览器使用获取到的 IP 地址与服务器建立 TCP 连接。建立 TCP 连接的过程中涉及一个握手过程。你需要进行几次网络往返才能完成这个过程。为了保持加载过程快速，现代浏览器使用一种称为“长连接”（keep-alive connection）的机制，尽量重复使用已建立的到服务器的 TCP 连接。需要注意的是，如果协议是 HTTPS，建立新连接的过程会更加复杂。它需要通过一个称为 SSL/TLS 握手的复杂过程，在浏览器和服务器之间建立加密连接。这个握手过程成本较高，因此浏览器会使用诸如 SSL 会话恢复（SSL session resumption）之类的技巧来尝试降低成本。最后，浏览器通过已建立的 TCP 连接向服务器发送 HTTP 请求。HTTP 本身是一个非常简单的协议。服务器处理请求后返回响应。浏览器接收响应并渲染 HTML 内容。通常，还需要加载其他资源，例如 JavaScript 包和图片。浏览器会重复我们上面提到的过程，执行 DNS 查找、建立 TCP 连接、发送 HTTP 请求，从而完成对其他所有资源的获取。如果你想了解更多关于系统设计的知识，可以看看我们的书籍和每周通讯。如果你学到了新知识，欢迎订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P2 How does HTTPS work

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=2)　时长 6分59秒

**原文**

Hi, welcome to another byte-by-go system design video. In this video, we'll talk about HTTPS. What is so important about HTTPS at these days, most websites require it. How does HTTPS work? We'll answer these questions in this video. Let's dive right in. Without HTTPS, the communication between the browser and the server is in plain text. This means that the password you enter or the credit card number you sent over in and that, can be read by anyone who has the ability to intercept it. HTTPS is designed to solve this problem, to make the data send over in and that unreadable by anyone other than the sender and the receiver. HTTPS is an extension of the HTTP protocol. We discussed HTTPS in our last video. Check the link down below if you like to learn more about it. With HTTPS, data is sending an encrypted form using something called TLS. TLS stands for transport layer security. If the encrypted data gets intercepted by a hacker, all they could see is jumbo data. This is where TLS handshake comes in. Let's take a look at how this handshake works. There are several steps. Step 1. Just like the case with HTTPS, the browser establishes a TCP connection with the server. Step 2. This is where the TLS handshake begins. The browser sends a client hello to the server. In this hollow message, the browser tells the server the following things. 1. What TLS versions it can support it could be TLS 1.2 TLS 1.3 so on and so forth. 2. What cybersecurity supports a cybersecurity is a set of encryption algorithms to use to encrypt data. After receiving the client hello, the server gets to choose the cybersecurity and the TLS version to use. Based on the options it got from the client. It sends those in a server hollow message back to the client. The server then sends a certificate to the client. The certificate includes a lot of different things. One of the key things is the public key for the server. The client uses the public key in something called asymmetric encryption. In asymmetric encryption, a piece of data that is encrypted by a public key can only be decrypted by the private key. We'll discuss how this is used in a bit. This concludes Step 2. The hollow face of the TLS handshake. At this point, the client has a server certificate and the client and a server have agreed on the TLS version and the cybersecurity to use. Now to step 3. This is to step work the client and the server come up with a share encryption key to use to encrypt data. This is the puzzle we talked about earlier. And this is what asymmetric encryption comes into picture. Again, with asymmetric encryption, the data encrypted on the client side using the public key from the server can only be decrypted by the server. This is how the client sends an encryption key safely to the server over the wide open internet. All this is done in the client key exchange message. This exact detail varies depending on the surface we used. Here we use RSA as an example since it is an easiest to understand. With RSA, the client generates an encryption key, also call a session key, and encrypts it with the server's public key and sends the encrypted session key to the server over the internet. The server receives the encrypted session key and decrypts it with this private key. So now the server has the session key too. Now both sides hold the session key and this is where the intercept four of the TLS handshake. Where they use the session key and agree upon Cypher Suite to send encrypted data back and forth in a secure bi-directional channel. Now you might ask, why don't we just use asymmetric encryption for everything? Why switch to a symmetric encryption at all? The main reason is that asymmetric encryption is computationally expensive. This is something suitable for bulk data transmission. Now before we close, there are two finer points I would like to discuss. First, the handshake we talked about applied to TLS 1.2. While the latest version of TLS is 1.3, and 1.3 is supported on all major browsers. As we can see in our illustration, TLS 1.2 takes two n° trips to complete. This is one of the major improvements of TLS 1.3. It optimizes the handshake to reduce a number of n° trip to 1. We decided to talk about TLS 1.2 because we reviewed TLS 1.3 as an optimization. As with most optimizations, it is a bit harder to explain. This one we chose TLS 1.2 instead. The core concept in TLS 1.2 still applied to 1.3. The second finer point we would like to discuss is that in the explanation about we used RSA for asymmetric encryption to securely exchange the symmetric session key. Again, we chose RSA because it is easy to understand. However, asymmetric encryption is not the only way to share the session key between the client and the server. In fact, the TLS 1.3 RSA is no longer supported as a method for key exchange. Tiffy Helman is a more common way nowadays for exchanging session keys. Tiffy Helman is complicated, but in a nutshell, it uses some advanced math involving large prime numbers to derive a share session key without ever transmitting a public key over the network. Well, that is it for HTTPS. You would like to learn more about system design, check out our books and weekly newsletter. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

你好，欢迎收看另一期“字节入门”系统设计的视频。在本视频中，我们将讨论 HTTPS。如今 HTTPS 为何如此重要？大多数网站都要求使用它。HTTPS 是如何工作的？我们将在本视频中解答这些问题。让我们直接开始吧。如果没有 HTTPS，浏览器与服务器之间的通信都是明文形式。这意味着你输入的密码或发送的信用卡号等信息，可能会被任何能够拦截通信的人读取。HTTPS 的设计初衷就是为了解决这个问题，确保传输的数据除了发送方和接收方之外，任何人都无法读懂。HTTPS 是 HTTP 协议的扩展。我们在上一个视频中已经讨论过 HTTPS，如果你想了解更多相关内容，可以查看下方的链接。使用 HTTPS 时，数据会通过一种名为 TLS 的技术进行加密传输。TLS 的全称是“传输层安全”（Transport Layer Security）。如果加密数据被黑客拦截，他们也只能看到一堆乱码。这时就轮到 TLS 握手发挥作用了。让我们来看看这个握手过程是如何进行的。整个过程包含多个步骤。步骤一：与 HTTPS 的情况类似，浏览器首先会与服务器建立 TCP 连接。步骤二：TLS 握手从这里开始。浏览器向服务器发送一条 client hello 消息。在这条消息中，浏览器会告知服务器以下信息：1. 浏览器支持的 TLS 版本，例如 TLS 1.2、TLS 1.3 等；2. 浏览器支持的密码套件。密码套件是一组用于加密数据的加密算法。收到 client hello 后，服务器会根据客户端提供的选项，选择要使用的密码套件和 TLS 版本，并将这些选择通过 server hello 消息返回给客户端。接下来，服务器会向客户端发送一张证书。证书包含许多不同的信息，其中关键的一项就是服务器的公钥。客户端会使用公钥进行一种称为非对称加密的操作。在非对称加密中，用公钥加密的数据只能由私钥解密。我们稍后会讨论它的具体应用方式。至此，TLS 握手的第二步完成。此时，客户端已获取服务器证书，且客户端与服务器已就 TLS 版本和密码套件达成一致。现在进入第三步。这是为了让客户端和服务器共同生成一个共享加密密钥，用于加密数据。这就是我们之前讨论过的难题。而这就是非对称加密发挥作用的地方。同样，在非对称加密中，客户端使用服务器的公钥加密的数据只能由服务器解密。客户端正是通过这种方式，在开放的互联网上安全地将加密密钥发送给服务器。所有这些操作都是在客户端密钥交换消息中完成的。具体细节因所使用的协议而异。这里我们以 RSA 为例，因为它最易于理解。在 RSA 中，客户端生成一个加密密钥（也称为会话密钥），并使用服务器的公钥对其进行加密，然后将加密后的会话密钥通过互联网发送给服务器。服务器收到加密后的会话密钥后，使用其私钥进行解密。这样，服务器现在也拥有了该会话密钥。此时，双方都持有会话密钥，TLS 握手协议的第四步就此展开。双方使用会话密钥并协商确定密码套件，以便在安全的双向通道中来回传输加密数据。现在你可能会问，为什么我们不直接使用非对称加密来处理一切呢？为什么还要切换到对称加密呢？主要原因是非对称加密的计算开销较大。它更适合用于大批量数据传输。在结束之前，还有两个细节我想讨论一下。首先，我们刚才提到的握手过程适用于 TLS 1.2。虽然最新的 TLS 版本是 1.3，并且 1.3 已得到所有主流浏览器的支持。从我们的示意图中可以看到，TLS 1.2 需要往返两次才能完成。这是 TLS 1.3 的主要改进之一。它优化了握手过程，将往返次数减少到一次。我们之所以选择讲解 TLS 1.2，是因为我们将 TLS 1.3 视为一种优化方案。和大多数优化一样，它解释起来稍微复杂一些。因此我们选择了 TLS 1.2。TLS 1.2 的核心概念仍然适用于 1.3。我们想讨论的第二个细节是，在之前的解释中，我们使用 RSA 作为非对称加密来安全地交换对称会话密钥。同样，我们选择 RSA 是因为它易于理解。然而，非对称加密并非客户端和服务器之间共享会话密钥的唯一方式。实际上，TLS 1.3 中的 RSA 已不再被支持为密钥交换方法。如今，Diffie-Hellman 是一种更常见的会话密钥交换方式。Diffie-Hellman 虽然较为复杂，但简而言之，它通过涉及大质数的先进数学方法推导出共享的会话密钥，而无需在网络上传输公钥。以上就是关于 HTTPS 的全部内容。如果您想进一步了解系统设计，欢迎查看我们的书籍和每周通讯。如果您学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P3 System Design_ Why is Kafka fast

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=3)　时长 5分2秒

**原文**

Why is Kafka fast? What is the secret? We'll talk about it in this video. Let's dive right in. We'll first start by acknowledging that the term fast is ambiguous. What does it even mean that Kafka is fast? Are we talking latency? Are we talking throughput? Is fast compared to what? Kafka is optimized for high throughput. It is designed to move a large number of records in a short amount of time. Think of it as a very large pipe moving the liquid. The bigger the diameter of the pipe, the larger the volume of liquid that can move through it. So when someone says Kafka is fast, that usually we refer to Kafka's ability to move a lot of data efficiently. What are some of the design decisions that help Kafka move a lot of data quickly? There are many design decisions that contributed to Kafka's performance. In this video, we'll focus on two. We think these two carry the most weight. The first one is Kafka's reliance on sequential IO. What is sequential IO? Let's stick deeper into that. There's a common misconception that this access is slow compared to memory access. But this largely depends on data access patterns. There are two types of this access patterns, random and sequential. For hard drives, it takes time to physically move the arm to different locations on the magnetic disks. This is what makes random access slow. For sequential access though, since your arm doesn't need to jump around, it is much faster to read and write blocks of data one after the other. Kafka takes advantage of this by using an append-only log as this primary data structure. An append-only log adds new data to the end of the file. This access pattern is sequential. Now let's bring this idea home with some numbers. On modern hardware with an array of this hard disks, sequential will write reach hundreds of megabytes per seconds. While random writes a measure in hundreds of kilobyte per second, sequential access is several order of magnitude faster. Using hard disks has its cost advantage too. Compared to SSD, hard disks come as one-third of the price, but with about three times the capacity. Giving Kafka a large pool of cheap, this space without any performance penalty, means that Kafka can cost effectively reaching messages for a long period of time. A feature that was uncommon to messaging system before Kafka. The second design choice that gives Kafka its performance advantage is its focus and efficiency. Kafka's moves a lot of data from network to disk and from disk to network. This critically important to eliminate excess copy when moving pages and pages of data between the disk and the network. Now this is where zero copy principle comes into the picture. Modern Unix operating system, a highly optimized transfer data from this to network without copying data excessively. Let's dive deeper to see how this is done. First we look at how Kafka sends a page of data on this to the consumer when zero copy is not used at all. First the data is loaded from disk to the OS cache. Second, the data is copied from the OS cache into the Kafka application. Third, the data is copied from Kafka to the socket buffer. And fourth, the data is copied from the socket buffer to the network interface called buffer. And finally the data is sent over the network to the consumer. Now this is clearly inefficient. There are four copies and two system calls. Now let's compare this to zero copy. The first step is the same. The data pages loaded from the disk to the OS cache with zero copy. The Kafka application uses a system call called send file to tell the operating system to directly copy the data from the OS cache to the network interface called buffer. In this optimized path, the only copy is from the OS cache into the network cart buffer. With a modern network cart, this copying is done with DMA. DMA stands for direct memory access. When DMA is used, the CPU is not involved, making it even more efficient. To recap, sequential IO and zero copy principle are the cornerstone to Kafka's high performance. Kafka uses other techniques to squeeze every ounce of performance of modern hardware. But these two are the most important in our view. If you'd like to learn more about system design, check out our books and weekly newsletter. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

为什么 Kafka 这么快？它的秘诀是什么？我们将在本视频中探讨这个问题。让我们直接开始吧。首先，我们要承认“快”这个说法是模糊的。Kafka 快到底意味着什么？我们是在谈论延迟吗？还是在谈论吞吐量？快是相对于什么而言的？Kafka 针对高吞吐量进行了优化。它被设计成在很短的时间内移动大量记录。可以把它想象成一根非常大的管道，用来输送液体。管道的直径越大，能够通过的液体体积就越多。因此，当有人说 Kafka 很快时，通常指的是 Kafka 能够高效地移动大量数据的能力。那么，有哪些设计决策帮助 Kafka 快速移动大量数据呢？有许多设计决策都对 Kafka 的性能做出了贡献。在本视频中，我们将重点介绍其中两个。我们认为这两个因素最为关键。第一个是 Kafka 对顺序 IO 的依赖。什么是顺序 IO？让我们深入探讨一下。一个常见的误解是，这种访问方式与内存访问相比很慢。但这在很大程度上取决于数据访问模式。数据访问模式有两种：随机访问和顺序访问。对于硬盘来说，将磁臂物理移动到磁盘中不同位置需要时间。这正是随机访问速度慢的原因。然而，对于顺序访问而言，由于磁臂不需要来回跳转，因此可以更快地理连续地读取和写入数据块。Kafka 利用这一点，采用“仅追加日志”作为其主要数据结构。仅追加日志会将新数据添加到文件末尾，这种访问模式是顺序的。现在，我们通过一些数字来具体说明这一点。在现代硬件上，如果使用一组这样的硬盘，顺序写入的速度可达每秒数百兆字节；而随机写入的速度则以每秒数百千字节为单位计算，顺序访问的速度要快上几个数量级。使用硬盘还具有成本优势。与 SSD 相比，硬盘的价格仅为三分之一，但容量却大约高出三倍。这使得 Kafka 能够拥有一个大量廉价的存储空间，而不会牺牲性能，从而可以经济高效地长期存储消息。这一特性在 Kafka 出现之前，对于消息系统而言并不常见。Kafka 性能优势的第二个设计选择在于其专注与高效。Kafka 大量地将数据从网络传输到磁盘，以及从磁盘传输到网络。这一点至关重要，因为它消除了当在磁盘和网络之间移动大量数据页时产生的多余拷贝。而这正是零拷贝（zero copy）原则发挥作用的地方。现代 Unix 操作系统能够高度优化地从磁盘到网络的数据传输，而不会过度拷贝数据。让我们深入探讨它是如何实现的。首先，我们来看在完全没有使用零拷贝的情况下，Kafka 如何将一个数据页发送给消费者。第一步，数据从磁盘加载到操作系统缓存中。第二步，数据从操作系统缓存拷贝到 Kafka 应用程序中。第三步，数据从 Kafka 拷贝到套接字缓冲区（socket buffer）。第四步，数据从套接字缓冲区拷贝到网络接口的发送缓冲区。最后，数据通过网络发送给消费者。显然，这种方式效率低下，共涉及四次拷贝和两次系统调用。现在，我们再将其与零拷贝进行比较。第一步是相同的：数据页从磁盘加载到操作系统缓存中，此时采用了零拷贝技术。Kafka 应用使用一个名为 `sendfile` 的系统调用，通知操作系统将数据直接从操作系统缓存复制到网络接口的缓冲区。在这种优化路径中，唯一的数据复制操作是从操作系统缓存复制到网络卡的缓冲区。对于现代网络卡而言，这种复制是通过 DMA 完成的。DMA 是“直接内存访问”（direct memory access）的缩写。当使用 DMA 时，CPU 不会参与其中，从而效率更高。总结一下，顺序 I/O 和零拷贝原则是 Kafka 高性能的基石。Kafka 还采用了其他技术，以充分挖掘现代硬件的每一分性能。但在我看来，这两者最为关键。如果您想进一步了解系统设计，可以查看我们的书籍和每周通讯。如果您学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P4 System Design How to store passwords in the database

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=4)　时长 3分45秒

**原文**

How do we store passwords safely in the database? How do we validate a password or user enter against what we store in the database? Let's take a look. It is essential to store passwords in a way that keeps an attacker from getting them, even if the database is compromised. Let's talk about what not to do first. Do not store passwords in plain text. Anyone with internal access to the database can see them. If the database is compromised, an attacker could easily get all the passwords. So what should we do to save restore password in the database? The open web application security project, also known as OWSP, provided some guidelines on how to store passwords. Here are the recommendations. First, use a modern hashing algorithm. Hashing is a one way function. It is impossible to decrip a hash to obtain the original value. If an attacker obtains the hashed password, they can not just enter it into the application to gain access. It is important to use a modern hashing function designed for to securely store passwords. These are sometimes characterized as slow functions that use more resources to compute. This makes brute-for-actex and attractive. Note that some common legacy hashing function like MD5 and SHA1 are fast. There are less secure and should not be used. Second, salt the passwords. According to the OWSP guidelines, a salt is a unique randomly generated strain that is added to each password as a part of the hashing process. Why do we need to salt the password at all? So storing a password as a one way hash is a step in the right direction, but it is not sufficient. An attacker can defeat one way hashes with pre-computation attacks. Some common attacks are rainbow tables and database-based lookups. With these techniques, a hacker could crack a password in seconds. By adding a salt that is unique to each password to the hashing process, it ensures that the hashes unique to each password. This simple technique makes pre-computation attacks unattractive. Let's take a look at how we store a password and is salt. We first combine the password provided by the user with the randomly generated salt. We then compute the hash of this combination using an appropriate hashing function. The hash is stored in the database along with the salt. Again, the salt is used to generate a unique hash. It is not a secret and can be stored safely as plain attacks in the database. Finally, let's take a look at the process in reverse. When the user locks in, how do we validate the password against what is stored in the database? First, we fetch the salt for the user from the database. We then append the salt to the password provided by the user and hash it. We compare this computer hash against the hash stored in the database. If they are the same, the password is valid. That's it about storing passwords in the database. You would like to learn more about system design. Check out our books and weekly newsletter. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

我们如何在数据库中安全地存储密码？如何验证用户输入的密码是否与数据库中存储的密码匹配？让我们一起来看看。以能够防止攻击者获取密码的方式存储密码至关重要，即使数据库遭到泄露也是如此。我们先来谈谈哪些做法是不可取的。不要以明文形式存储密码。任何拥有数据库内部访问权限的人都能看到这些密码。如果数据库被攻破，攻击者就能轻易获取所有密码。那么，我们应该如何在数据库中安全地存储密码呢？开放网络应用安全项目（Open Web Application Security Project），即 OWASP，提供了一些关于如何存储密码的指导方针。以下是其建议：首先，使用现代哈希算法。哈希是一种单向函数，无法通过哈希值反推出原始值。即使攻击者获取了哈希后的密码，也无法直接将其输入应用程序以获得访问权限。因此，使用专为安全存储密码而设计的现代哈希函数非常重要。这类函数通常被称为“慢速函数”，因为它们需要更多的计算资源。这使得暴力破解攻击变得不再具有吸引力。请注意，一些常见的旧式哈希函数（如 MD5 和 SHA1）速度很快，但安全性较低，不应再使用。其次，应对密码进行加盐（salt）。根据 OWASP 指南，盐（salt）是一种在哈希过程中添加到每个密码中的、随机生成的唯一字符串。我们为什么需要对密码加盐呢？仅将密码以单向哈希形式存储是朝着正确方向迈出的一步，但这还不够。攻击者可以通过预计算攻击破解单向哈希。一些常见的攻击方式包括彩虹表（rainbow tables）和基于数据库的查找。利用这些技术，黑客可以在几秒钟内破解密码。通过在哈希过程中为每个密码添加唯一的盐，可以确保每个密码生成的哈希值都是唯一的。这一简单的技术使得预计算攻击不再具有吸引力。下面我们来看看如何存储密码及其盐。首先，我们将用户提供的密码与随机生成的盐进行组合。然后，使用合适的哈希函数计算该组合的哈希值。最终，将该哈希值与盐一起存储在数据库中。再次强调，盐的作用是生成唯一的哈希值。这并非秘密，可以安全地以明文形式存储在数据库中。最后，我们再反过来看看整个过程。当用户锁定账户时，我们如何验证密码与数据库中存储的密码是否匹配？首先，我们从数据库中获取用户的盐值。然后，将盐值追加到用户提供的密码之后并进行哈希处理。接着，我们将计算出的哈希值与数据库中存储的哈希值进行比较。如果两者相同，则密码有效。以上就是关于在数据库中存储密码的全部内容。如果您想进一步了解系统设计，欢迎查看我们的书籍和每周通讯。如果您学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P5 Big Misconceptions about Bare Metal, Virtual Machines, and Containers

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=5)　时长 7分2秒

**原文**

Hi, welcome to another System Design video. What are the differences between band metal, virtual machines and containers? When deploying a modern application stack, how do we decide which one to use? In this video, we'll take a closer look at each of these, let's dive right in. The granddaddy of these is band metal. A band metal, server is a physical computer that is single, ten and only. Once upon a time, all servers were band metal. Band metal gives us complete control over the hardware resources and the software stack to run. For software applications that require the absolute highest performance from the hardware, band metal could be a good way to go. Band metal servers are physically isolated. The isolation provides two benefits. First, it is not affected by the noisy neighbor problem. This problem occurs when one tenant's performance is impacted because of the activities of another tenant sharing the same hardware. Second, isolation provides the highest level of security. For example, it is not affected by side channel attacks. These attacks take advantage of the design flaws in modern micro processors to allow a malicious tenant to steal secrets from its neighbors. This wrong security guarantee is another reason to use band metal. When an application needs to meet the most stringent security, compliance or regulatory requirements, band metal can sometimes be the only way to go. Now what are the downsides of band metal? Band metal is expensive, hard to manage and hard to scale, acquiring new hardware takes time and it takes a competent team to manage them well. Next up is virtual machines, a virtual machine is the emulation of a physical computer. This is called virtualization. Many virtual machines can run on a single piece of band metal hardware. On top of the band metal hardware is the host operating system. Running on top of the host operating system is a special piece of software called a hypervisor. Now this is also known as a virtual machine monitor. The hypervisor manages virtual machines, it creates an abstraction layer over the hardware so that multiple operating systems can run alongside each other. Each virtual machine has its own gas operating system and on top of each gas operating system runs the applications for a tenant. As the sign up, there is a concept called bare metal hypervisor, not to be confused with bare metal hardware though. A bare metal hypervisor controls the hardware directly, without relying on the host operating system. This gives the hypervisor full control over the hardware and provide higher performance. However, hardware that supports the bare metal hypervisor is more expensive. Virtual machines have come a long way in performance in scale. These days we can choose virtual machines of pretty much any size to fit our workloads. In general, purpose ones would just a few CPU cores and a few gigabytes of memory to the high performance ones with hundreds of cores and terabytes of memory. Now what are the benefits of virtual machines? Virtual machines are cheaper to run, many of them can share the same hardware, allowing much higher resource utilization. They are easier to scale, too. This provides an organization more flexibility. Setting up virtual machines is easier, some fancy virtualization software can even move a running virtual machines from one bare metal hardware to another without shutting down the virtual machine. Now what are some of the downsides to virtual machines? Virtual machines could be vulnerable to the noisy neighbor problem described earlier. If our application co-locates with a resource hog of a neighbor, our own application performance could suffer. Also, virtual machines running on the same bare metal hardware share the same physical CPU cores. They are vulnerable to attacks that aim at design flaws in modern microprocessors. Side channel attacks like meltdown and spectre are some well-known examples. Now, next up, containers. A container is a lightweight and standalone package of an application with all its dependencies like libraries, frameworks and is one time. A containerization is considered to be lightweight version of virtualization. Leverialization here, we have bare metal hardware and the host operating system. But instead of a virtualizing hardware with a hypervisor, we virtualized the operating system itself with a piece of special software called the container engine. On top of the container engine runs many containers. Each of these is its own application environment, isolated from each other. The container engine provides even faster resource provisioning, and all the resources needed to run the application a package together so that the applications can run anywhere. Containers are scalable and portable, they are lightweight and require less hardware resources to run than virtual machines. A bare metal server can host significantly more containers than virtual machines. Since each container runs as a native process of the host operating system, they are much faster to start too. All these may contain us even easier to deploy and maintain a scale. However, containers are potentially less secure. They share the same underlying operating system and the isolation relies on the OS level primitives. This means container, I expose to a wider class of security vulnerability at the operating system level. It is possible to run containers inside virtual machines. Why would we want to do that though? Well, one reason is that it provides better security by reducing the possible attack services. Now, this is a trade-off between flexibility and security. What comes off the containers? Well, serverless and edge computing come to mind. They make the developer productivity and deployment stories even more compelling, but with their own sets of trade-offs. We may log into the yeast in another video. In conclusion, system design is about trade-offs. It is no different when it comes to bare metal, virtual machines and containers. There's no single right answer. If you would like to learn more about system design, check out our books and weekly newsletter. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

你好，欢迎收看另一期系统设计的视频。裸金属、虚拟机和容器之间有什么区别？在部署现代应用栈时，我们该如何决定使用哪一种？在本视频中，我们将深入探讨每一种技术，现在就开始吧。这些技术中的鼻祖是裸金属。裸金属服务器是一台物理计算机，专用于单一租户。曾几何时，所有服务器都是裸金属。裸金属让我们能够完全控制硬件资源和软件栈来运行软件应用。对于那些需要从硬件获取极致性能的软件应用来说，裸金属可能是一个不错的选择。裸金属服务器在物理上是隔离的。这种隔离带来了两个好处。首先，它不会受到“吵闹邻居”问题的影响。当某个租户的性能因共享同一硬件的其他租户的活动而受到影响时，就会出现这个问题。其次，隔离提供了最高级别的安全性。例如，它不会受到侧信道攻击的影响。这类攻击利用了现代微处理器的设计缺陷，使恶意租户能够窃取其邻居的机密信息。这种错误的安保保障是使用专用硬件的另一个原因。当应用程序需要满足最严格的安保、合规或监管要求时，专用硬件有时是唯一的选择。那么，专用硬件有哪些缺点呢？专用硬件价格昂贵、难以管理且难以扩展，获取新硬件需要时间，并且需要一个称职的团队才能妥善管理。接下来是虚拟机，虚拟机是对物理计算机的模拟。这被称为虚拟化。许多虚拟机可以在单台专用硬件上运行。在专用硬件之上是主机操作系统。在主机操作系统之上运行着一款特殊的软件，称为监控程序（hypervisor）。现在，它也被称为虚拟机监视器。监控程序负责管理虚拟机，它在硬件之上创建了一个抽象层，使得多个操作系统可以并行运行。每台虚拟机都有自己的客户操作系统，而在每台客户操作系统之上则运行着租户的应用程序。在注册过程中，有一个叫做“裸金属超管理器”（bare metal hypervisor）的概念，不过它不应与“裸金属硬件”（bare metal hardware）相混淆。裸金属超管理器直接控制硬件，而不依赖主机操作系统。这使得超管理器能够完全掌控硬件，并提供更高的性能。然而，支持裸金属超管理器的硬件价格更为昂贵。虚拟机在性能扩展方面已经取得了长足的进步。如今，我们可以选择几乎任何大小的虚拟机来满足我们的 workload。通常，通用型虚拟机可能只需要几个 CPU 核心和几 GB 内存，而高性能型虚拟机则可能配备数百个核心和 TB 级内存。那么，虚拟机有哪些优势呢？运行虚拟机的成本更低，多个虚拟机可以共享相同的硬件，从而实现更高的资源利用率。它们也更容易扩展，这为组织提供了更大的灵活性。设置虚拟机也更加便捷，一些先进的虚拟化软件甚至可以在不关闭虚拟机的情况下，将正在运行的虚拟机从一台裸金属硬件迁移到另一台。那么，虚拟机又有哪些劣势呢？虚拟机可能面临之前所描述的“吵闹邻居”问题。如果我们的应用与一个资源占用过高的邻居应用共存，我们自身应用的性能可能会受到影响。此外，运行在相同裸金属硬件上的虚拟机共享相同的物理 CPU 核心，因此它们容易受到针对现代微处理器设计缺陷的攻击。侧信道攻击（如 Meltdown 和 Spectre）就是其中一些广为人知的例子。接下来是容器。容器是一种轻量级且独立的应用程序包，包含了其所有依赖项（如库、框架等），且只需构建一次。容器化被认为是虚拟化的一种轻量级版本。在虚拟化中，我们有裸金属硬件和宿主机操作系统。但不同于通过监视程序对硬件进行虚拟化，容器化则是通过一种称为容器引擎的特殊软件对操作系统本身进行虚拟化。在容器引擎之上运行着多个容器。每个容器都是一个独立的应用程序环境，彼此相互隔离。容器引擎提供了更快的资源分配速度，并将运行应用程序所需的所有资源打包在一起，从而使应用程序可以在任何地方运行。容器具有可扩展性和可移植性，它们轻量级，运行所需的硬件资源比虚拟机更少。一台裸金属服务器可以托管比虚拟机多得多的容器。由于每个容器都作为主机操作系统的原生进程运行，因此启动速度也要快得多。所有这些因素都可能使我们更容易地部署和维护扩展。然而，容器在安全性方面可能存在不足。它们共享相同的底层操作系统，其隔离性依赖于操作系统级别的原始机制。这意味着容器会暴露于更广泛的操作系统的漏洞之中。可以在虚拟机内部运行容器。但为什么我们要这样做呢？原因之一是这可以通过减少潜在的攻击面来提高安全性。现在，这是在灵活性和安全性之间的一种权衡。容器之外还有什么呢？无服务器和边缘计算便浮现在脑海中。它们让开发人员的生产力和部署故事更具说服力，但也伴随着各自的权衡。我们可能会在另一个视频中深入探讨酵母。总之，系统设计就是关于权衡。在裸机、虚拟机和容器方面也是如此。没有唯一正确的答案。如果你想了解更多关于系统设计的信息，可以看看我们的书籍和每周通讯。如果你学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P6 FAANG System Design Interview_ Design A Location Based Service (Yelp, Google Pla

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=6)　时长 24分41秒

**原文**

Hi. Welcome to another System Design video. In this video we design a proximity service. What is a proximity service? This is a service that is commonly found as a back-and-component in an app like Yelp to find the best restaurants nearby or in a mapping app to find the closest gas stations. Let's first understand the problem and establish a design school. Different apps may have different requirements for the proximity service. In this video we designed this service as part of the business review app like Yelp. These apps have many features when it comes to searching for nearby businesses. There's not feasible to design all of them in this video. We'll narrow down the functional requirements to its core feature set. Here are what we came up with. One, given a user's location in a search radius as inputs, we turn all businesses within the search radius. Two, businesses owners can add, delete or update a business. These changes don't need to be appear in real-time. It is okay for the changes to appear the next day. Three, users of the app can view detailed information about a business. Next, it is important to discuss the non-functional requirements. Non-functional requirements place various constraints on the design. These requirements make the design interesting and challenging. Let's assume this review app has 100 million DAU and 200 million businesses. What are some of the important non-functional requirements based on this scale? We came up with the following. One, the latency should be low. Users should be able to find nearby businesses quickly. Two, the service should be highly available. It should be able to handle traffic spikes during peak hours. Now, let's do some back of the envelope calculations based on the design constraints of 100 million DAU and 200 million businesses. You should give us a rough idea about some of the design challenges and trade-offs down the road. We need to make one more assumption in order to do this math, though. For daily active user on average, how many search queries do they make? Now, five queries per day seems like a reasonable number. So, let's use that. Here we have two dimensional data. The data set returned for each dimension is huge. We could retrieve all businesses within a longitude range or a latitude range, but to fetch businesses within a search radius, we need to find the intersection of those ranges. This is inefficient because each data sets still contains a lot of data. Looking at it, another way, the root problem with this approach is that the index can only improve the search speed in one of the two dimensions. Naturally, the follow-up question is this. Can we map two dimensional data into one dimension? So we can build a single index on it. The answer is yes. There are two general approaches when it comes to geospatial indexing. The first approach is hash-based. Some examples are even grit and geohash. The second approach is tree-based. These include Quartree and Google S2. Even though the underlying implementations of these two brought approaches are different, the high level idea is the same. They divide the map into small areas and build indexes for fast search. Let's explore the hash-based solutions first. One simple approach is to evenly divide the world into smaller grids. One grid could have many businesses and each business belongs to one grid. This approach has one major issue. The distribution of businesses is uneven. There could be lots of businesses in downtown New York, for example. While in real areas there could be very few businesses. Ideally, we want to use more granular grids for dense areas and larger grids for sparse ones. Geohash is an improvement to the evenly divided grid. It works by reducing the two-dimensional location data into a one-dimensional string of letters and digits. What does that really mean? Let's go over how geohash works at a high level. First, it divides the world into four quadrants along the prime meridian and equator. These four quadrants are represented by two bits. For the first bit, the left half is zero and the right half is one. For the second bit, the top half is one and the bottom half is zero. Next, it divides each grid into four smaller grids. Each smaller grid is again represented by another two bits. The bits for the sub-grids are dependent to the existing bits. It repeats the subdivision and keeps adding more bits to the geohash. It stops subdividing when the sub-grid reaches the desired size. Here is an example for the grid that contains the Google headquarters. In this case, the grid size is 1200 meters by 600 meters. It's not using the long series of ones and zeros to represent a geohash. It is encoded as a base 32 string. Here, the long binary number is converted to a base 32 string with six letters. Now, to look at it from another angle, the base 32 encoders string length determines the size of the grid. These are called precision or levels and there are 12 of these levels. For the location-based service, we only interested in length 4 to 6. For anything longer than 6, the grid size is too small. It is smaller than 4, the grid size is too large. Now, how do we choose to write precision given a search radius? We want to find a minimal geohash length that covers the whole circle. For example, if the radius is 2 kilometers, the geohash length should be 5. Another nice property is that geohash is just a string. Searching all businesses within a geohash is very simple. It means that any database could work. We don't need to use a special geospatial database. We'll talk about how to use geohash in a relational database in a little bit. Geohash works great most of the time, but there are some edge cases we should discuss. These edge cases have to do with how boundaries are handled. If two geohash strings share long prefix, we know they are close. For example, here are some grits that share the prefix 9kxzn. However, the reverse is not true. Two grits could be right next to each other, but have no shared prefix at all. This is because the grits are either side of the equator, or the prime meridian, are in the different halves of the world. For example, used to French cities are only 30 kilometers apart, but their geohashes share no common prefix. Another boundary issue is that two locations can have a long share prefix, but they are in two different geohashes. In this example, the green and red dots share five letters of the geohash strings, yet they are in two different grits. A common solution to both of these issues is to fashion businesses not only within the current grid, but also from its eight neighbors. Calculating the neighboring geohashes is not difficult. They are libraries to handle that in constant time. This concludes the exploration of hash-based geoh special indexing. Next we will briefly look at the tree-based implementation. In this video, we will not implement the location-based service using any tree-based index, but it is good to understand at a high level how they work. A common tree-based index is a quad tree and Google S2. A quad tree is a data structure that partitions a two-dimensional space by recursively subdividing it into four quadrants. The subdivision stops when the grits meet certain criteria. In our use case, the criterion can be to keep subdividing until the number of businesses in the grid is no more than some number, say 100. A key point for quad tree and any other tree-based indexes for their matter is that it is an in-memory data structure. It is not a database solution. This means that the index is built by our own code and runs on the location-based servers. This is very different approach with a different set of deployment and operational constraints. For our design, we will focus on using geohash instead. Okay, so back to geohash. We want the use geohash as the geospatial index to speed up the location search. We don't need a special geospatial database for that. The geohash column contains the geohash of the right precision for each business. Converting a location in latitude and longitude to a geohash is straightforward. There are plenty of libraries to handle that. Two things we should discuss with this table schema. First, many businesses will share the same geohash. This means different business IDs within a geohash are storing different database rows. And the geohash and business ID columns together form a compound key. With the compound key, it makes removal of a business from the table efficient. Second, as we discussed earlier, for our application, we are interested in geohash with precision 4 to 6. They correspond to the different search radii from 0.5 kilometers to about 20 kilometers. The geohash column in the table will all have a geohash precision of 6. We will use the light operator in SQL to search for shorter prefix links. Now, he is an example. With this query, we find everything that is within a geohash of 9Q8ZN. Next, let's talk about how to scale the geospatial index table. Do we actually need to scale beyond one database for this table? Well, let's do some quick math. For this table, each geohash is 6 characters long. Let's assume that the business ID is 64 bits, so that's egg bites. Optionally, we may also want to store latitude and longitude for each business, so that it is easy to calculate the distance of each business from the user. So let's add another 16 bytes to it. So each row is about 30 bytes. With 200 million businesses, the total table size is about 6 GB. This is tiny for modern hardware. The entire dataset can easily fit in a single database server. However, our recapias of 5,000 is quite high. With peak time spikes, a single database may not have enough CPU or network bandwidth to handle all the read requests. Now, what are some of the ways we can spread the read low? You are two general approaches. We can add read replicas or we can charge the database. Shorting is more complicated. It requires shorting logic in the application layer. In our case, the entire dataset can fit in a single machine. There is no strong reason to short. A better approach for us is to have a series of read replicas of the geospatial index table to help with the read low. This method is much easier to maintain than shorting. The last point we would like to touch on is caching. Do we need a caching layer in a design? It is not obvious if caching is a win. Our workload is read heavy and the geospatial dataset is small. As we just discussed above, if read performance is the bottleneck, we can add read replicas to help. However, there is another dataset in our design. It is the business table. We estimated earlier that the dataset for the business table with 200 million businesses is in a terabyte range. For modern hardware, it is not that large. For this video, we will omit the caching layer. Let's recap by tracing a life of a search request to our final design. Bob tries to find restaurants within 500 meters. 你好。欢迎收看另一期系统设计视频。在本视频中，我们将设计一个邻近服务。什么是邻近服务？这是一种常见于类似 Yelp 的应用程序中的后端组件，用于查找附近的最佳餐厅；或在地图应用中查找最近的加油站。让我们首先理解问题并确定设计目标。不同的应用程序对邻近服务可能有不同的需求。在本视频中，我们将该服务设计为类似 Yelp 的商业评论应用的一部分。这类应用在搜索附近商家方面具有多种功能。在本视频中，我们无法将所有功能都设计出来。因此，我们将功能需求缩小到其核心功能集。以下是我们得出的结论：第一，给定用户的位置和搜索半径作为输入，返回搜索半径范围内的所有商家。第二，商家所有者可以添加、删除或更新商家信息。这些更改无需实时显示，即使第二天才生效也可以接受。第三，应用用户可以查看商家的详细信息。接下来，讨论非功能性需求非常重要。非功能性需求对设计施加了各种约束。这些需求使设计变得有趣且富有挑战性。假设这款点评应用拥有1亿日活跃用户（DAU）和2亿家商户。基于这一规模，有哪些重要的非功能性需求呢？我们总结出以下几点：第一，延迟必须低。用户应能快速找到附近的商户。第二，服务必须具备高可用性。它应能应对高峰时段的流量激增。现在，我们基于1亿DAU和2亿商户的设计约束，进行一些粗略估算。这能让我们对未来的设计挑战和权衡有一个大致的了解。不过，为了进行这些计算，我们还需要做一个假设：平均而言，每个日活跃用户每天会发起多少次搜索查询？每天五次似乎是一个合理的数字，我们就采用这个数值。据此，我们可以计算出该服务的每秒搜索查询量。我们有1亿DAU，每个DAU每天进行五次搜索，因此每秒的搜索查询量约为5,000次。现在，我们还需要大致了解需要多少存储空间来容纳2亿家企业。我们将在更清楚地了解数据模式后再进行这项计算。既然我们已经对这项服务的需求有了相当清晰的认识，接下来让我们看看高层级设计。在本节中，我们将探讨API设计、数据模式，然后逐步讲解高层级设计图。首先，我们来看看这项服务的API设计。这里我们采用Restful API规范，这样可以保持简单。根据我们之前讨论的功能需求，API大致分为两类。第一类是用于搜索附近企业。这是一个单一的GET请求，输入参数为用户所在位置的纬度和经度，以及一个可选的搜索半径。该API会返回一个包含多个企业对象的数组，以及企业总数。这里有两点需要注意。首先，为了简化这个简短视频的内容，我们省略了分页功能。但在实际应用中，我们应该在API设计中包含分页功能。其次，API响应中的企业对象仅包含足以呈现搜索结果的信息。它不包含足够的信息来呈现企业的详情页。为了呈现企业详情页以及其他所需的 API 端点，我们进入了另一个 API 类别。第二个 API 类别负责管理企业对象，它包含用于创建、读取、更新和删除企业的 CRUD 端点。这部分对于 API 来说相当标准，我们不会深入探讨。接下来，我们来看看该服务的数据库模式。这里有两个关键的数据库表。第一个是企业表，它存储了关于企业的详细信息。主键是企业 ID。该表支持我们刚才讨论的企业 API 的 CRUD 操作。表结构相当直观。第二个表则更有趣一些，它被企业搜索查询 API 用来快速返回附近的企业。该表至少需要包含企业的 ID 和位置信息。这些位置必须通过高效的方式建立索引，以便快速生成搜索结果。选择这种表结构和数据库需要一定的地理空间搜索知识。我们稍后会深入探讨这一点。既然我们已经讨论了模式，现在应该能够做一些粗略的计算和存储需求估算。那么到目前为止我们知道些什么呢？这里有2亿家企业。对于企业表，我们估计每家企业的所有详细信息不会超过10千字节。平均而言，可能更接近1千字节。因此，企业表的大小应该在低太字节范围内。那么用于附近搜索的表会怎么样呢？这个表应该包含每家企业的ID、纬度和经度。这些字段每个都是8字节。大约是2亿乘以24字节，也就是大约5千兆字节。这表明用于位置搜索的数据集非常小。当数据库如此小时，我们有很多设计选择，包括那些完全基于内存的方案。现在我们已经有足够的信息来绘制一个粗略的顶层设计图了。在这个系统中，有两个端口。它们各自映射到前面提到的一个API类别。我们把第一部分称为基于位置的服务，第二部分称为企业服务。让我们快速看一下这个顶层设计中的各个组件。首先是负载均衡器。现在你来设计负载均衡器，根据 API 路由将传入流量分配到两个服务。我们稍后再看。这两个服务都是无状态的。负载均衡器后部署无状态服务的情况非常常见，我们在此不会深入探讨。请注意，在现实世界中，我们所在的公司可能采用其他方式来将流量分配给服务，比如 Envoy、Kubernetes 集群，或 AWS 上的 API 网关。具体细节并不那么重要。负载均衡器后面是我们之前提到的两个服务。基于位置的服务是该系统的核心部分，它能根据给定的搜索半径和位置快速找到附近的商家。基于位置的服务有几个值得关注的特性：第一，它以读操作为主，没有任何写请求；第二，QPS 很高，我们之前的预估是 5,000 QPS；第三，该服务是无状态的，应该很容易进行水平扩展。接下来是商家服务。商家服务是负载均衡器后的另一个服务，负责处理管理商家对象的相关请求。API 的创建、更新和删除部分的 QPS 并不高。根据我们的功能需求，这些更改也无需立即生效。这为我们提供了很大的设计灵活性。不过，对于读取操作，高峰时段的 QPS 可能会很高。由于这些数据不会频繁变化，因此如有必要，它们将是缓存的绝佳候选对象。关于缓存的更多内容稍后讨论。接下来，在高层设计图中是数据库集群。我们尚未讨论将使用哪种类型的数据库。我们将在深入探讨部分再回到这个话题。然而，基于访问模式和读写查询量，我们已经掌握了足够的信息来初步确定要使用的数据库类型。让我们总结一下目前已知的信息：服务以读取为主；QPS 远高于 QPS；写入操作无需立即生效；搜索数据集非常小；业务数据集稍大一些，我们可能需要对其进行优化，稍后我们会再讨论这一点。基于这些观察，使用主从架构的数据库集群似乎是一个不错的起点。在这种配置中，主数据库处理所有写请求，而读副本则处理大量的读请求。数据首先保存到主数据库，然后复制到副本。由于复制延迟，数据会存在一定程度的一致性差异，但对我们来说，业务并不需要实时反映这些变化，因此这种不一致性问题不大。至此，我们已经对整个系统有了一个清晰的了解。接下来，让我们深入探讨几个值得关注的领域。首先，我们来讨论一下使用哪种数据库来支持基于位置的服务。有一类被称为地理空间数据库（geospatial databases）的数据库，它们专为存储和查询几何空间中的数据（即位置数据）而优化。这类数据库的一些示例包括 Redis 的 Geo 模块，以及带有 PostGIS 扩展的 PostgreSQL。为了从宏观上理解这些数据库的工作原理，我们稍作展开，探讨一下支撑这些数据库的地理空间索引背后的两种常见算法。这些知识将有助于我们为生产环境选择合适的解决方案。最直观但效率较低的方法是画一个圆，然后找出圆内的所有商户。这些伪 SQL 查询向我们展示了如何在数据库中实现类似功能。这个类的结构看起来有点复杂，但它基本上会查找位于该类指定的边界框内的所有商家。显而易见的问题是，这个查询效率不高。它需要扫描包含 2 亿条记录的表，因此速度很慢。那么，我们该如何改进呢？一种思路是在经度和纬度列上建立索引。但这并没有让查询效率提升太多。让我们看看问题出在哪里。这里的数据是二维的，每个维度返回的数据集都非常庞大。我们可以检索出特定经度范围或纬度范围内的所有商家，但要获取特定搜索半径内的商家，就需要找出这些范围的交集。这种方法效率低下，因为每个数据集仍然包含大量数据。换个角度来看，这种方法的根本问题在于索引只能提升两个维度中某一个维度的搜索速度。很自然地，接下来的问题是：我们能否将二维数据映射到一维？这样我们就可以基于这个一维数据建立单个索引。答案是肯定的。在地理空间索引方面，主要有两种通用方法。第一种是基于哈希的方法，例如 even grit 和 geohash。第二种是基于树的方法，包括 Quartree 和 Google S2。尽管这两种方法的基础实现不同，但其高层理念是一致的：它们都将地图划分为更小的区域，并建立索引以实现快速搜索。我们先来探讨基于哈希的解决方案。一种简单的方法是将整个世界均匀地划分为更小的网格。一个网格可以包含多个企业，而每个企业都属于某一个网格。这种方法存在一个主要问题：企业的分布并不均匀。例如，纽约市中心可能有大量企业，而在一些偏远地区，企业可能寥寥无几。理想情况下，我们希望为密集区域使用更精细的网格，为稀疏区域使用更大的网格。Geohash 是对均匀划分网格的一种改进。它通过将二维位置数据转换为一个由字母和数字组成的一维字符串来实现这一目标。这究竟意味着什么呢？让我们从整体上了解一下 geohash 的工作原理。首先，它将世界沿着本初子午线和赤道划分为四个象限。这四个象限由两位二进制位表示。对于第一位，左半部分为 0，右半部分为 1。对于第二位，上半部分为 1，下半部分为 0。接下来，它将每个网格划分为四个更小的网格。每个更小的网格同样由另外两位二进制位表示。子网格的二进制位取决于已有的二进制位。它会重复这种细分过程，并持续向 geohash 中添加更多的二进制位。当子网格达到所需大小时，细分过程便会停止。以下是一个包含谷歌总部的网格示例。在这种情况下，网格尺寸为 1200 米乘以 600 米。它并未使用一长串 1 和 0 来表示 geohash，而是将其编码为 base32 字符串。此处，这个长长的二进制数被转换为一个包含六个字母的 base32 字符串。现在，从另一个角度来看，base32 编码字符串的长度决定了网格的大小。这些被称为“精度”或“层级”，共有 12 个层级。对于基于位置的服务而言，我们只关心长度为 4 到 6 的情况。对于任何超过 6 的情况，网格尺寸太小。如果小于 4，网格尺寸又太大。那么，给定一个搜索半径，我们该如何选择精度呢？我们希望找到一个能够覆盖整个圆的最小 geohash 长度。例如，如果半径是 2 公里，geohash 长度应为 5。geohash 的另一个优点是它只是一个字符串。在某个 geohash 范围内搜索所有商家非常简单。这意味着任何数据库都能胜任，我们无需使用专门的地理空间数据库。稍后我们将讨论如何在关系型数据库中使用 geohash。大多数情况下，geohash 的表现都非常出色，但也有一些边缘情况需要讨论。这些边缘情况与边界处理方式有关。如果两个 geohash 字符串共享一个较长的前缀，我们知道它们彼此接近。例如，这里有一些共享前缀 9kxzn 的网格。但反过来并不成立：两个网格可能彼此紧邻，却没有任何共享前缀。这是因为这些网格可能分别位于赤道或本初子午线的两侧，处于世界的不同半球。例如，法国的一些城市之间相距仅30公里，但它们的geohash却没有共同的前缀。另一个边界问题是，两个位置可能有很长的共同前缀，但它们却属于不同的geohash。在本例中，绿色和红色的点在geohash字符串中共享了五个字母，但它们却位于两个不同的网格中。解决这两个问题的一个常见方法是，不仅在当前网格内开展业务，还要考虑其周围的八个相邻网格。计算相邻的geohash并不难，有专门的库可以在常数时间内处理这一任务。至此，基于哈希的地理特殊索引的探索就到此结束。接下来，我们将简要了解一下基于树的实现方式。在本视频中，我们不会使用任何基于树的索引来实现基于位置的服务，但了解它们的基本工作原理还是很有帮助的。一种常见的基于树的索引是四叉树（quad tree）和Google S2。四叉树是一种数据结构，它通过将二维空间递归地划分为四个象限来进行分区。当网格满足特定标准时，这种划分过程就会停止。在我们的用例中，判断标准可以是持续细分，直到网格中的企业数量不超过某个数值（例如 100）。对于四叉树以及任何其他基于树的索引来说，一个关键点在于它们都是内存数据结构，而非数据库解决方案。这意味着索引由我们自己的代码构建，并在基于位置的服务器的本地运行。这是一种截然不同的方法，具有不同的部署和运行约束。对于我们的设计，我们将重点使用 geohash。好的，回到 geohash。我们希望使用 geohash 作为地理空间索引，以加快位置搜索的速度。那么，我们该如何构建地理空间索引表呢？这是核心所在。该表只需要两列：geohash 和 business ID。任何关系型数据库都能处理 geohash，我们无需为此使用专门的地理空间数据库。geohash 列包含每个企业对应精度的 geohash。将经纬度坐标转换为 geohash 的过程非常直接，市面上有许多库可以处理这项工作。针对这个表结构，我们有两点需要讨论。首先，许多企业可能会共享相同的 geohash。这意味着，同一个 geohash 内的不同业务 ID 会存储不同的数据库行。geohash 和业务 ID 这两列共同构成了一个复合键。借助这个复合键，从表中删除某个业务的操作会非常高效。其次，正如我们之前讨论的，对于我们的应用来说，我们关注的是精度为 4 到 6 的 geohash。这些精度对应着从 0.5 公里到约 20 公里的不同搜索半径。表中的 geohash 列都将采用 6 的精度。我们将在 SQL 中使用前缀匹配操作符来搜索较短的前缀。现在来看一个例子。通过这条查询，我们可以找到位于 geohash 9Q8ZN 范围内的所有数据。接下来，我们来谈谈如何扩展地理空间索引表。对于这个表，我们真的需要将数据扩展到单个数据库之外吗？让我们快速计算一下。对于这个表，每个 geohash 的长度为 6 个字符。我们假设业务 ID 是 64 位，也就是 8 个字节。此外，我们可能还需要为每个业务存储纬度和经度，以便轻松计算每个业务与用户之间的距离。因此，我们再增加 16 个字节。这样，每一行大约占用 30 个字节。拥有2亿家企业，总表大小约为6 GB。对于现代硬件来说，这非常小。整个数据集可以轻松地容纳在单个数据库服务器中。然而，我们5,000次的查询请求量相当高。在高峰时段，单个数据库可能没有足够的CPU或网络带宽来处理所有的读请求。那么，有哪些方法可以分散读负载呢？主要有两种通用方法：我们可以添加读副本，或者对数据库进行分片。分片更为复杂，它需要在应用层添加分片逻辑。在我们的情况下，整个数据集可以容纳在单台机器中，因此没有充分的理由进行分片。对我们而言，更好的方法是为地理空间索引表创建一系列读副本，以帮助处理读负载。这种方法比分片更容易维护。最后一点我们想讨论的是缓存。在设计中是否需要缓存层？缓存是否带来收益并不明显。我们的负载以读为主，且地理空间数据集很小。正如我们刚才讨论的，如果读性能是瓶颈，我们可以添加读副本来帮助。然而，我们的设计中还有另一个数据集，那就是企业表。我们之前估计，包含2亿家企业的商业表数据集大约为1TB。对于现代硬件而言，这个规模并不算太大。该数据集的大小处于一个临界点，此时进行分片是有意义的。对于这张表来说，更新速度较慢，而读取操作较多。如果我们为其添加一个缓存层，应该可以不用分片。该缓存可以处理大部分对高频访问企业的读取请求，同时只要为商业表预留出足够的扩展空间即可。增加读副本也有助于缓解读取压力。针对商业表，我们有多种选择。我们的建议是，首先使用单个数据库上线。随着产品的增长，借助良好的监控手段，我们可以根据增长和使用模式做出反应，之后再决定是否进行分片、添加读副本或在表前增加缓存层。在本视频中，我们将省略缓存层。让我们通过追踪一次搜索请求的流程来总结一下最终设计：Bob尝试查找500米范围内的餐厅。客户端将位置和搜索半径发送给负载均衡器。负载均衡器将请求转发给基于位置的服务。根据位置和搜索半径，该服务会找到与搜索请求相匹配的地理哈希精度。500米对应长度为6的地理哈希。服务会计算相邻的地理哈希。总共将有9个地理哈希需要与地理空间索引表进行查询。服务向数据库发送查询，以获取这些地理哈希范围内的业务ID及其经纬度对。服务使用这些经纬度对计算用户与各个业务之间的距离。我们对其进行排序，并将结果返回给客户端。以上就是邻近度服务的设计。我们希望了解更多关于系统设计的内容，欢迎查看我们的书籍和每周通讯。如果您学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢。我们下次再见。

## P7 Scan To Pay in 2 Minutes

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=7)　时长 2分20秒

**原文**

Hi, welcome to another System Design video. In this video, we'll discuss scan to pay. This method of payment is available via digital wallet apps, Nik, Pay, Pay, Power, and Vimal. By scanning a dynamically generated QR code at the corner sale terminal. How does it work? Let's take a look. To understand how it works, we need to divide a scan to pay process into two parts. The first part is for the merchant to generate the QR code and display it on screen. The second part is for the consumer to scan the QR code and pay. Let's take a look at the steps for generating the QR code. When we're ready to pay, the cachiers clicks to check out button. The cachiers computer sends the total amount and order ID to the payment service provider. The PSP saves this information to the database and generates a QR code URL. The PSP returns the QR code URL to the cachiers computer. The cachiers computer sends the QR code to the checkout terminal. The checkout terminal displays the QR code. All these steps complete within a second. Now, the consumer pays from the digital wallet by scanning the QR code and here are the steps involved. The consumer opens the digital wallet app and scans the QR code. The total amount to pay is display in the app after confirming the amount the consumer clicks to pay button. The wallet app notifies the PSP that the given QR code has been paid. The PSP marks the QR code as paid and returns a success message to the wallet app. The PSP notifies the merchant that the consumer has paid to given QR code. This is how to pay using a dynamic QR code. That is dynamic because the QR code is generated for one-time use. This is also possible to pay by scanning the printer QR code at the merchant. This is called static QR code. It works a bit differently and we might cover it in a different video. You will like to learn more about system design, check out our books and weekly newsletter. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

你好，欢迎收看另一期系统设计视频。在本视频中，我们将讨论“扫码支付”。这种支付方式可通过 Nik、Pay、Pay、Power 和 Vimal 等数字钱包应用实现，只需扫描收银台终端上动态生成的二维码即可。它是如何运作的呢？让我们一起来看看。要理解其原理，我们需要将“扫码支付”流程分为两个部分：第一部分是商家生成二维码并显示在屏幕上；第二部分是消费者扫描二维码并完成支付。我们先来看看生成二维码的步骤。当我们准备付款时，收银员会点击“结账”按钮。收银员的电脑将总金额和订单 ID 发送给支付服务提供商（PSP）。PSP 将这些信息保存到数据库，并生成一个二维码 URL。随后，PSP 将该二维码 URL 返回给收银员的电脑。收银员的电脑再将二维码发送到收银终端，终端随即显示该二维码。所有这些步骤都在一秒内完成。接下来，消费者通过数字钱包扫描二维码进行支付，具体步骤如下：消费者打开数字钱包应用并扫描二维码。在消费者确认金额并点击支付按钮后，应用中会显示应付总额。钱包应用会通知 PSP，指定的二维码已完成支付。PSP 将该二维码标记为已支付，并向钱包应用返回成功消息。PSP 随后通知商家，消费者已通过该二维码完成支付。这就是使用动态二维码付款的方式。之所以称为动态，是因为该二维码仅用于一次性支付。消费者也可以通过扫描商家处的打印机二维码来完成支付，这种方式被称为静态二维码。它的运作方式略有不同，我们可能会在另一期视频中介绍。如果您想进一步了解系统设计，欢迎查看我们的书籍和每周简报。如果您学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P8 Consistent Hashing Algorithms Every Developer Should Know For System Design

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=8)　时长 8分4秒

**原文**

What did DynamoDB, Apache Cassandra, Discord and ECMI CDN have you in common? They all use consistent hashing. Now, what is consistent hashing? Why do all the cool kids use it? In this video, we'll learn all about it. Let's dive right in. In a large scale distributed system, data does not fit on a single server. They are distributed across many machines. This is called horizontal scaling. To build such a system with predictable performance, it is important to distribute the data evenly across those servers. A common method to distribute data as evenly as possible amount of servers is simple hashing. This is how it works. First, for each object, we hashings keys with a hashing function, like MD5 or murmur hash. This maps the object key to a known range of numerical values. A good hashing function, distributes the hashes evenly across the entire range. Second, we perform the marginal operation on the hash against a number of servers. This determines which servers the object belongs to. As long as the number of servers stays the same, an object key will always map to the same server. Here's a concrete example. We have four servers with eight string keys. With simple hashing, this is how we distribute the eight string keys amount of the four servers. This approach works well when the size of the cluster is fixed, and the data distribution is even. But what happens when new servers get added to meet new demand or when existing servers get removed? Back to our example, if server 1 goes down, the size of the cluster is now 3. Even though the hashes for the object key stays the same, we are now applying the marginal operation to a different set of n. In this case, it is now 3. The impact is pretty drastic. Most of the keys get redistributed. This affects almost all objects. It's not just the objects originally stored in the server that is now offline. This triggers a storm of misses and lots of objects to be moved. For situations where servers constantly come and go, this design is untenable. Consistent hashing is an effective technique to mitigate this issue. The goal of consistent hashing is this. We want almost all objects to stay assigned to the same server, even as the number of objects changes. Here is the core insight of consistent hashing. In addition to hashing the object keys like before, we also have to server names. The objects and servers are hashed with the same hashing function to the same range of values. In our example, we have a range of x0 to xn. This range is called a hash space. Next, we connect both ends of the hash space to form a ring. This is a hash ring. Using a hashing function, we hash each server by its name or IP address, and place the server onto the ring. Here we place our four servers onto the ring. Next, we hash each object by its keys with the same hashing function. A nice simple hashing where we perform a modular operation on the hash. Here we use the hash directly to map the object key onto the ring. Here's what we will look like for our four objects. To locate the server for a particular object, we go clockwise from the location of the object key on the ring, until a server is found. Continue with example, key zero is on server zero, and key one is on server one. Now, let's take a look at what happens when we add a server. Here we insert a new server s4 to the left of s0 on the ring. Note that only k0 needs to be moved from s0 to s4. This is because s4 is the first server at k0 and counters. By going clockwise, some k0's position on the ring. Keys, k1, k2 and k3 are not affected. With simple hashing, when a new server is added, almost all the keys need to be remapped. With consistent hashing, adding a new server only requires a distribution of a fraction of the keys. Let's walk through a quick example of removing a server. When s1 is removed, only k1 needs to be remapped to s2. The rest of the keys are unaffected. Let's recap what have we learned so far. One, we map both servers and objects onto the hash ring using a uniformic distributed hash function. Two, to locate a server for an object, we go clockwise on the ring from the object's position until a server is found. Now let's consider a potential issue with this design. The distribution of the objects in a server on the ring is likely to be uneven. Conceptually, we pick and random points on the ring. We are very unlikely to get a perfect partition of the ring into equally sized segments. For example, if servers are map on the ring like this, most of the objects are stored in s2 with s1 and s3 storing no data. This problem gets worse if servers come and go frequently. In our example, even if the servers were originally evenly spaced, if s1 is removed, the segment for s2 is now twice as large as the ones for s0 and s3. Virtual notes are used to fix this problem. The idea is to have each server appear at multiple locations on the ring. Each location is a virtual note representing a server. In this hash ring, we have two servers with each having three virtual notes. Instead of having s0 and s1, we now have s0 0, s0 1, and s0 2 to represent server 0. And s1 0, s1 1 and s1 2 to represent server 1 on the ring. With virtual notes, each server handles multiple segments on the ring. In our example, the segments label s0 are managed by server 0, and those label s1 are handled by server 1. In real world systems, the number of virtual notes is much larger than 3. As the number of virtual notes increases, the distribution of objects becomes more balanced. Having more virtual notes means taking more space to store the metadata about the virtual notes. This is a trade-off, and we can tune the number of virtual notes to fit our system requirements. Let's see how consistent hashing is used in the real world. Some popular notes equal databases like Amazon DynamoDB and Apache Cassandra use consistent hashing, where it is used for data partitioning. It helps these databases minimize data movement during rebalancing. Content delivery networks like Accomi use consistent hashing to help distribute web contents evenly amounted to edge servers. Low balancers like Google low balancers use consistent hashing to distribute persistent connections evenly across back and servers. This limits the number of connections that need to be reestablished when a back and server goes down. That's it for consistent hashing. You would like to learn more about system design, check out our books and weekly newsletters. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

DynamoDB、Apache Cassandra、Discord 和 ECMI CDN 有什么共同点？它们都使用了一致性哈希。那么，什么是一致性哈希？为什么所有“酷孩子”都在用它？在本视频中，我们将全面了解它。让我们直接开始吧。在大规模分布式系统中，数据无法存储在单台服务器上，而是分布在多台机器上。这被称为横向扩展。为了构建一个性能可预测的系统，将数据均匀地分布在这些服务器上非常重要。一种尽可能均匀地将数据分布到各台服务器的常用方法是简单哈希。它的原理如下：首先，对于每个对象，我们使用哈希函数（如 MD5 或 Murmur Hash）对键进行哈希。这将对象键映射到一个已知的数值范围内。一个好的哈希函数会将哈希值均匀地分布在整个范围内。其次，我们对哈希值对服务器数量进行取模运算，从而确定该对象属于哪台服务器。只要服务器数量保持不变，一个对象键将始终映射到同一台服务器。下面是一个具体示例：我们有四台服务器和八个字符串键。通过简单的哈希算法，我们可以将八个字符串键分配到四台服务器上。当集群规模固定且数据分布均匀时，这种方法效果良好。但是当为了满足新需求而添加新服务器，或者当现有服务器被移除时，会发生什么呢？回到我们的例子，如果服务器 1 宕机，集群规模现在变为 3。尽管对象键的哈希值保持不变，但我们现在要对不同的 n 值执行取模运算。在这种情况下，n 现在为 3。其影响非常巨大——大多数键都会被重新分配，这几乎会影响到所有对象。不仅仅是原本存储在已离线服务器上的对象会受到影响，这还会引发大量的缓存未命中，并导致大量对象被迁移。对于服务器频繁增减的场景，这种设计是不可行的。一致性哈希是一种有效缓解该问题的技术。一致性哈希的目标是：即使对象数量发生变化，也尽可能让几乎所有对象继续分配给同一台服务器。这就是一致性哈希的核心思想：除了像之前一样对对象键进行哈希外，我们还需要对服务器名称进行哈希。对象和服务器都使用相同的哈希函数哈希到相同的值范围内。在我们的示例中，我们有一个从 x0 到 xn 的范围。这个范围被称为哈希空间。接下来，我们将哈希空间的两端连接起来形成一个环。这就是哈希环。我们使用一个哈希函数，通过每个服务器的名称或 IP 地址对其进行哈希处理，然后将服务器放置到环上。这里我们将四个服务器放置到环上。接下来，我们使用相同的哈希函数，通过每个对象的键对其进行哈希处理。这是一种简单直接的哈希方式，我们对哈希值执行取模运算。这里我们直接使用哈希值将对象键映射到环上。以下是我们四个对象的情况。要找到特定对象对应的服务器，我们从对象键在环上的位置开始顺时针移动，直到找到一台服务器。继续看示例，键零位于服务器零上，键一位于服务器一上。现在，我们来看看添加服务器时会发生什么。这里我们在环上 s0 的左侧插入一台新服务器 s4。注意，只有 k0 需要从 s0 移动到 s4。这是因为 s4 是 k0 遇到的第一台服务器。通过顺时针移动，会改变某些 k0 在环上的位置。键 k1、k2 和 k3 不受影响。在简单的哈希方案中，当添加一台新服务器时，几乎所有的键都需要被重新映射。而在使用一致性哈希时，添加一台新服务器仅需重新分配一部分键。我们来快速看一个移除服务器的例子。当 s1 被移除时，只有 k1 需要被重新映射到 s2，其余的键均不受影响。让我们总结一下到目前为止所学到的内容。第一，我们使用均匀分布的哈希函数将服务器和对象都映射到哈希环上。第二，为了找到某个对象对应的服务器，我们从该对象的位置开始沿环顺时针方向查找，直到找到一台服务器为止。现在，我们来考虑这种设计可能存在的一个问题。服务器上对象的分布在环上很可能是不均匀的。从概念上讲，我们在环上随机选取了一些点，但几乎不可能将环完美地划分成大小相等的区段。例如，如果服务器在环上的映射方式如下所示，那么大部分对象都会存储在 s2 中，而 s1 和 s3 则没有存储任何数据。如果服务器频繁地加入和离开，这个问题会变得更加严重。在我们的示例中，即使服务器最初是均匀分布的，如果 s1 被移除，那么 s2 的扇区现在会比 s0 和 s3 的扇区大一倍。虚拟节点被用来解决这一问题。其核心思想是让每台服务器在环上以多个位置出现。每个位置都是一个代表该服务器的虚拟节点。在这个哈希环中，我们有两个服务器，每个服务器拥有三个虚拟节点。我们不再只有 s0 和 s1，而是用 s0 0、s0 1 和 s0 2 来表示服务器 0，并用 s1 0、s1 1 和 s1 2 来表示环上的服务器 1。通过虚拟节点，每台服务器可以管理环上的多个扇区。在我们的示例中，标记为 s0 的扇区由服务器 0 管理，而标记为 s1 的扇区则由服务器 1 管理。在实际系统中，虚拟节点的数量远不止 3 个。随着虚拟节点数量的增加，对象的分布会变得更加均衡。增加虚拟节点意味着需要占用更多空间来存储关于这些虚拟节点的元数据。这是一个权衡取舍，我们可以根据需要调整虚拟节点的数量以满足系统要求。让我们来看看一致性哈希在现实世界中的应用。一些流行的分布式数据库（如 Amazon DynamoDB 和 Apache Cassandra）使用一致性哈希（consistent hashing）进行数据分区。这有助于这些数据库在重新平衡期间最大限度地减少数据移动。内容分发网络（如 Accomi）使用一致性哈希来均匀地将 Web 内容分发到边缘服务器。负载均衡器（如 Google 负载均衡器）使用一致性哈希将持久化连接均匀地分配到后端服务器。这减少了当某个后端服务器宕机时需要重新建立连接的数量。以上就是关于一致性哈希的全部内容。如果您想进一步了解系统设计，欢迎查看我们的书籍和每周通讯。如果您学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P9 Why is single-threaded Redis so fast

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=9)　时长 3分39秒

**原文**

Why is Reddit so fast? What fundamental design decisions did the developers make more than a decade ago? That's true to test of time. Let's take a look. Red is a very popular in memory database. It's rock solid, easy to use, and fast. These attributes explain why it is one of the most loved databases, according to the Stack Overflows Annual Developer Survey. The first reason Red is fast is because it is an in memory database. Memory access is several orders of magnitude faster than random this I.O. Pure memory access provides high read and write throughput and low latency. The trade-off is that the data set cannot be larger than memory. Co-wise, in memory data structures are also much easier to implement than on this counterparts. This keeps the code simple, and it contributes to Red's rock solid stability. Another reason Red is fast is a bit unintuitive. It is primarily single threaded. Why would a single threaded design lead to high performance? Wouldn't it be faster if it uses thread to leverage all the CPU course? A multi-threaded applications require locks or other synchronization mechanisms. There are notoriously a hard to reason about. In many applications, the added complexity is bug-prong and sacrifices stability. Making it difficult to justify the performance gain. In the case of Reds, the single threaded code path is easy to understand. How does the single threaded code base handle many thousands of incoming requests and outgoing responses at the same time? Wouldn't the thread get blocked, rating for the completion of each request individually? Now, this is where I.O. multiplexing comes into the picture. With I.O. multiplexing, the operating system allows a single thread to weigh a many socket connections simultaneously. Traditionally, this is done with the select or pull system course. These system course are not very performant when there are many thousands of connections. On Linux, E-Pull is a performance variant of I.O. multiplexing that supports many many thousands of connections in constant time. A drawback of this single threaded design is that it does not leverage all the CPU cores available in modern hardware. For some workloads, it is not uncommon to have several reds instances running on a single server to utilize more CPU cores. We alluded to the third reason why Reds is fast. Since Reds is an in-memory database, it could leverage several efficient low-level data structures, without worrying about how to persist them to this efficiently. Linkless, skipless, and hash table are some examples. It is true that there are attempts at implementing new Reds compatible servers to squeeze more performance of a single server. With Reds' ease of use, rock solid stability and performance, it is in our view that Reds still provides the best performance and stability trade-off in the market. You like to learn more about system design, check out our books and weekly newsletter. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

为什么 Reddit 这么快？十多年前的开发者们做出了哪些根本性的设计决策？这些决策经受住了时间的考验。让我们一探究竟。Redis 是一款非常流行的内存数据库。它稳定可靠、易于使用且速度飞快。根据 Stack Overflow 年度开发者调查，这些特性使其成为最受喜爱的数据库之一。Redis 速度快的第一个原因是它是一款内存数据库。内存访问速度比随机磁盘 I/O 快几个数量级。纯粹的内存访问提供了高读写吞吐量和低延迟。其权衡之处在于，数据集的大小不能超过内存容量。此外，内存数据结构也比磁盘上的对应结构更容易实现。这使得代码保持简洁，并有助于 Redis 的稳定可靠。Redis 速度快的另一个原因有些反直觉：它主要是单线程的。为什么单线程设计反而能带来高性能？难道使用多个线程来利用所有 CPU 核心不是更快吗？多线程应用需要锁或其他同步机制，而这些机制往往难以理解和推理。在许多应用中，增加的复杂性容易引发错误，并牺牲稳定性，这使得性能提升难以被证明是合理的。就 Reds 而言，其单线程代码路径非常易于理解。那么，单线程代码库如何同时处理成千上万的传入请求和传出响应呢？难道线程不会因逐个等待每个请求完成而被阻塞吗？这时，I/O 多路复用就派上了用场。通过 I/O 多路复用，操作系统允许单个线程同时监听多个套接字连接。传统上，这是通过 `select` 或 `poll` 系统调用实现的。当连接数量达到成千上万时，这些系统调用的性能并不理想。在 Linux 上，`epoll` 是 I/O 多路复用的一种高性能变体，能够在常数时间内支持数以万计的连接。这种单线程设计的一个缺点是它无法利用现代硬件中所有的 CPU 核心。对于某些工作负载，在一台服务器上运行多个 Reds 实例以利用更多 CPU 核心并不少见。我们刚才已经提到了 Reds 速度快的第三个原因。由于 Reds 是一种内存数据库，它可以利用多种高效的底层数据结构，而无需担心如何高效地将它们持久化。无链接结构、无跳跃结构和哈希表都是其中的例子。确实有人尝试实现新的 Reds 兼容服务器，以进一步提升单台服务器的性能。凭借 Reds 的易用性、极佳的稳定性和出色的性能，我们认为 Reds 仍然是市场上性能与稳定性平衡得最好的选择。如果您想进一步了解系统设计，欢迎查看我们的书籍和每周通讯。如果您学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P10 HTTP1 to HTTP2 to HTTP3

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=10)　时长 4分7秒

**原文**

XDTP has been around since the 90s, how has it evolved over the years, what is the latest development? Let's take a look. He is the progression of the XDTP version so far, from XDTP 1 to 1.1 to 2 and 2 now 3. Let's look at each one more closely. XDTP 1 came out in 1996, it is built on top of TCP, every request to the same server requires a separate TCP connection. XDTP 1.1 soon followed in 1997, it introduced a keep alive mechanism so a connection could be reused for more than a single request. The persistent connections we do is request latency, because the client does not need to initiate expensive, TCP 3 way handshake for every request. This worthwhile to note that XDTP 1.1 also added XDTP pipelining. This in theory allows the client to send multiple requests before waiting for each response. The response must be received in the same order as two requests. It was tricky to implement correctly and many proxy servers in between did not handle pipelining properly. Its support was eventually removed from many web browsers. XDTP 1.1 with pipelining also suffers from an issue called head of line blocking. We've had a line blocking, subsequent requests on the same connection must wait for the previous requests to complete. If a request is blocked for any reason like packet loss, or subsequent requests on the same connection are also impacted. To keep loading performance at an acceptable level, browsers normally keep multiple TCP connections to the same server and send requests to it in parallel. XDTP 2 was published in 2015. XDTP 2 introduces XDTP streams, where multiple streams of requests could be sent to the same server on a single TCP connection. Unlike XDTP 1.1 pipelining, each stream is independent of each other and it does not need to be sent or received in order. XDTP 2 solves the head of line blocking issue at the application layer, but the issues still exist in the transport layer with TCP. Also note that XDTP 2 introduces a push capability to allow servers to send updates to the clients whenever new data is available with that requiring a client to pull. XDTP 3 began as a draft in 2020 and has recently been published in June 2022. It uses a new protocol called Quick, instead of TCP as the underlying transport protocol. Quick is based on UTP, introduces streams as the first class citizen at the transport layer. Quick streams share the same quick connections, so no additional handshix that required to create new ones. Quick stream at the liver independently. In most cases, packet loss affecting one stream doesn't affect others. This is how Quick eliminates the head of line blocking at the transport layer. Quick is designed for mobile heavy internet usage. People carrying smartphones, consonants, which from one network to another as they move about their day, will TCP the hand of a one connection from one network to another is sluggish. Quick implements a concept called connection ID, which allows connections to move between IP addresses and network interfaces quickly and reliably. Even though HTTP 3 has just been standardized, it is used by 25% of the websites and supported by many web browsers. You would like to learn more about system design, check out our books and weekly newsletter. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

XDTP 自 90 年代问世以来，经历了怎样的演变？最新进展如何？让我们一探究竟。以下是 XDTP 版本迄今为止的演进历程：从 XDTP 1 到 1.1，再到 2，如今已更新至 3。下面逐一详细分析。

XDTP 1 发布于 1996 年，它基于 TCP 构建，每次向同一服务器发起请求都需要建立独立的 TCP 连接。1997 年，XDTP 1.1 紧随其后推出，引入了长连接机制（keep alive），使同一连接可被多次请求复用。这种持久化连接降低了请求延迟，因为客户端无需为每个请求都执行耗时的 TCP 三次握手。值得注意的是，XDTP 1.1 还新增了 XDTP 流水线功能（pipelining）。理论上，这允许客户端在等待单个响应前连续发送多个请求，但响应必须按请求顺序返回。由于正确实现这一机制颇具挑战性，且许多中间代理服务器无法妥善处理流水线请求，最终许多浏览器移除了对该功能的支持。此外，启用流水线的 XDTP 1.1 还存在一种名为“队头阻塞”（head of line blocking）的问题。我们曾遇到过线路阻塞问题，即同一连接上的后续请求必须等待前面的请求完成。如果某个请求因数据包丢失等原因被阻塞，同一连接上的后续请求也会受到影响。为了将加载性能维持在可接受的水平，浏览器通常会与同一服务器保持多个 TCP 连接，并并行发送请求。XDTP 2 于 2015 年发布。XDTP 2 引入了 XDTP 流，允许多个请求流通过单个 TCP 连接发送到同一服务器。与 XDTP 1.1 的流水线技术不同，每个流彼此独立，无需按顺序发送或接收。XDTP 2 在应用层解决了线路头部阻塞问题，但在传输层的 TCP 中该问题仍然存在。此外，XDTP 2 还引入了推送功能，使服务器能够在新数据可用时主动向客户端发送更新，而无需客户端主动拉取。XDTP 3 最初于 2020 年以草案形式提出，最近已于 2022 年 6 月发布。它采用了一种名为 Quick 的新协议，取代了 TCP 作为底层传输协议。Quick 基于 UTP，在传输层将流（stream）提升为第一等公民。Quick 流共享相同的 Quick 连接，因此无需额外的握手即可创建新连接。每个 Quick 流独立运行。在大多数情况下，一个流的丢包不会影响其他流。这就是 Quick 在传输层消除队首阻塞（head-of-line blocking）的方式。Quick 专为移动设备主导的互联网使用场景而设计。对于随身携带智能手机、在一天中不断从一个网络切换到另一个网络的用户来说，TCP 在从一个网络切换到另一个网络时重新建立连接的过程非常缓慢。Quick 实现了一种名为“连接 ID”（connection ID）的机制，使连接能够快速可靠地在不同 IP 地址和网络接口之间迁移。尽管 HTTP/3 刚刚完成标准化，但已有 25% 的网站在使用它，并且它也得到了众多网页浏览器的支持。如果您想进一步了解系统设计，欢迎查看我们的书籍和每周简报。如果您学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P11 What Is REST API_ Examples And How To Use It

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=11)　时长 5分21秒

**原文**

Rest is the most common communication standard between computers over in and out. What is it? Why is it so popular? Let's take a look. API stands for application programming interface. It is a way for two computers to talk to each other. The common API standard used by most mobile and web applications to talk to the servers is called Rest. It stands for Representational State Transfer. It is a mouthful. What does that mean? Rest is not a specification. It is a loose set of rules that has been the common standard for building web API since the early 2000s. An API that follows the Rest standard is called a Restful API. Some real life examples are Twilio, Stripe and Google Maps. Let's look at the basics of Rest. Restful API organizes resources into a set of unique URIs or uniform resource identifiers. The URIs differentiate different types of resources on a server. Here are some examples. The resources should be grouped by now and not verb. An API to get all products should be slash products and not slash get all products. A client interacts with a resource by making a request to the endpoint for the resource over HTTP. The request has a very specific format as shown here. The line contains a URI for the resource we like to access. The URI is preceded by an HTTP verb which tells the server what we want to do with the resource. A post-request means we want to create a new resource. Again, means we want to read the data about an existing resource. A put is for updating an existing resource. A delete is for removing an existing resource. You might have heard the acronym CRUT. This is what it stands for. In the body of these requests, there could be an optional HTTP request body. That contains a custom payload of data usually encoded in JSON. The server receives a request, processes it, and formats a result into a response. The first line of the response contains a HTTP status code to tell the client what happened to the request. Now, our well-implemented RESTful API returns proper HTTP status codes. The 200-level codes mean the request was successful. The 400-level codes mean something was wrong with our request. For example, the request contains incorrect syntax. At a 500-level, it means something ran wrong at a server level. For example, the service was unavailable. A well-behaved client could choose to retry a fail request with a 500-level status code. We said could choose to retry because some actions are not identical and those require extra care when we try. When an API is identical, making multiple identical requests has the same effect as making a single request. This is usually not the case where it posts requests to create a new resource. The response body is optional and could contain the data payload and is usually formatted in JSON. There's a critical attribute of REST that is worth discussing more. A REST implementation should be stateless. It means the two parties don't need to restore any information about each other. And every request and response is independent from all others. This leads to web applications that are easy to scale and well-behaved. There are two final points to discuss to run a well-behaved RESTful API. If an API endpoint returns a huge amount of data, use pagination. They come in pagination scheme, use limit, and offset as parameters. Here's an example. If they are not specified, the server should assume sensible default values. Lastly, versioning of an API is very important. Versioning allows an implementation to provide backward compatibility so that if we introduce breaking changes from one version to another, consumers can get enough time to move to the next version. There are many ways to version an API. The most straightforward is to prefix the version before the resource on a URL, for instance, like this. RESTful API is simple and effective when applied sensibly. It may not be the best choice for all companies, but it is simple and good enough. And that's why it is so widely used. There are other popular API options like GraphQL and GRPC. We'll discuss those and compare them in separate videos. You'll like to learn more about system design, check out our books and weekly newsletter. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

“Rest” 是计算机之间进行输入和输出通信时最常见的标准。它是什么？为什么如此流行？让我们一探究竟。API 是应用程序编程接口（application programming interface）的缩写。它是两台计算机之间相互通信的一种方式。大多数移动和 Web 应用程序用于与服务器通信的通用 API 标准被称为 Rest。它代表“表述性状态转移”（Representational State Transfer）。这个名称听起来有点拗口。它到底是什么意思呢？Rest 并不是一项具体的规范，而是一套松散的规则，自 21 世纪初以来一直是构建 Web API 的通用标准。遵循 Rest 标准的 API 被称为 Restful API。一些现实生活中的例子包括 Twilio、Stripe 和 Google Maps。让我们来看看 Rest 的基础知识。Restful API 将资源组织为一组唯一的 URI（统一资源标识符）。这些 URI 用于区分服务器上的不同类型的资源。以下是一些示例。资源应按名词而非动词进行分组。获取所有产品的 API 应为 `/products`，而不是 `/get-all-products`。客户端通过向资源的端点发送 HTTP 请求来与资源交互。该请求具有非常特定的格式，如此处所示。该行包含一个我们想要访问的资源 URI。该 URI 前面有一个 HTTP 动词，用于告诉服务器我们希望对该资源执行什么操作。post 请求表示我们希望创建一个新资源。get 请求表示我们希望读取现有资源的数据。put 请求用于更新现有资源。delete 请求用于删除现有资源。你可能听说过 CRUT 这个缩写词，它代表的就是这些操作。在这些请求的请求体中，可以有一个可选的 HTTP 请求体，其中包含自定义的数据负载，通常以 JSON 格式编码。服务器接收到请求后，会对其进行处理后，将结果格式化为响应。响应的第一行包含一个 HTTP 状态码，用于告知客户端请求的处理结果。现在，我们实现良好的 RESTful API 会返回正确的 HTTP 状态码。200 级别的代码表示请求成功。400 级别的代码表示我们的请求存在问题。例如，请求的语法不正确。500 级别的代码则表示服务器端出现了问题。例如，服务不可用。一个行为良好的客户端可以选择重试状态码为 500 级别的失败请求。我们之所以说“可以选择重试”，是因为某些操作并非幂等操作，在尝试重试时需要格外小心。当 API 是幂等的时候，多次发送相同的请求与发送一次请求的效果相同。但在用于创建新资源的 POST 请求中，通常并非如此。响应体是可选的，可以包含数据负载，且通常采用 JSON 格式。REST 还有一个值得深入讨论的关键特性：REST 实现应当是无状态的。这意味着双方无需保留任何关于对方的信息，每一次请求和响应都独立于其他所有请求和响应。这使得 Web 应用更易于扩展且行为更稳定。要运行一个行为良好的 RESTful API，最后还有两点需要讨论。如果 API 端点返回的数据量巨大，请使用分页。分页方案通常使用 `limit` 和 `offset` 作为参数。示例如下：如果这些参数未被指定，服务器应使用合理的默认值。最后，API 版本控制非常重要。版本控制允许实现提供向后兼容性，这样当我们在不同版本之间引入破坏性更改时，消费者就有足够的时间迁移到下一个版本。为 API 版本化有多种方式。最直接的方法是在 URL 的资源前添加版本前缀，例如如下所示。当合理应用时，RESTful API 简单而有效。它可能并非所有公司的最佳选择，但足够简单且实用。这正是它被广泛使用的原因。其他流行的 API 选项还包括 GraphQL 和 GRPC。我们将在单独的视频中讨论并比较这些技术。如果您想了解更多关于系统设计的知识，欢迎查看我们的书籍和每周通讯。如果您学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。
