## P24 What Is A CDN_ How Does It Work

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=24)　时长 4分24秒

**原文**

What is the CDN? Why should we develop a large advantage of it? Let's take a look. CDN or content delivery network has been around since the late 90s. It was originally developed to speed up the delivery of static HTML content for users all around the world. CDN has evolved over the ensuing decades. Nowadays a CDN should be used whenever HTTP traffic is served. What can a modern CDN do for us? A plastic a closer look at a fundamental level, a CDN brings content closer to the user. This improves the performance of a web service as perceived by the user. It is well documented that performance is critical to user engagement and retention. To bring a service closer to the users, CDN deploy servers at hundreds of locations all around the world. These server locations are called point of presence or pops. A server inside the pop is now commonly called an edge server. Having many pops over the world ensures that every user can reach a fast edge server close to them. Different CDNs use different technologies to direct a user's request to the closest pop. To common ones, a DNS-based routing and any cast. With DNS-based routing, each pop has its own IP address. When a user looks up the IP address for the CDN, DNS returns the IP address of the pop closest to them. With any cast, all pops share the same IP address. When a request comes into the any cast network for that IP address, the network sends the request to the pop that is closest to the request. Each edge server acts as a reverse proxy with a huge content cache. We talked about reverse proxy in an earlier video. Check out the description if you like to learn more. Set a contents a cache at the edge server in the content cache. If a piece of content is in the cache, it could be quickly returned to the user. Since the edge server only asks for a copy of the static content from the origin server, it is not in the cache. This greatly reduces the low and bandwidth requirements of the origin server cluster. The modern CDN could also transform static content into more optimized formats. For example, you could modify JavaScript bundles on the fly or transform an image file from an old format to a modern one like WebP or AVIF. The S-Server also serves a very important role in the modern XDDP stack, or TLS connections terminate at the edge server. TLS handshix expensive. The company used TLS version like TLS 1.2 takes several network-run trips to establish. By terminating the TLS connection at the edge, its significantly reduces the latency for the user to establish an encrypted TCP connection. This is one reason why many modern applications send even dynamic and cacheable HTTP content over the CDN. Besides performance, a modern CDN brings two other major benefits, first is security. All modern CDNs have huge network capacity at the edge. This is the key to providing effective D-DOS protection against large-scale attacks. By having a network with capacity much larger than their attackers. This is especially effective with a CDN built on an any-cass network. It allows the CDN to diffuse the attack traffic over a huge number of servers. Second, a modern CDN improves availability. A CDN by its very nature is highly distributed. By having copies of contents available in many pops, a CDN can withstand many more hardware failures than the origin servers. A modern CDN provides many benefits. It will be a serving HTTP traffic we should be using a CDN. You would like to learn more about system design, check out our books and free weekly newsletter. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

什么是 CDN？为什么我们应该充分利用它的优势？让我们一起来看看。CDN（内容分发网络）自 20 世纪 90 年代末就已存在。它最初是为了加快向全球用户交付静态 HTML 内容而开发的。在过去的几十年里，CDN 不断发展。如今，只要涉及 HTTP 流量服务，就应该使用 CDN。现代 CDN 能为我们做些什么？从基础层面来看，CDN 将内容更靠近用户。这能提升用户感知到的网络服务性能。众所周知，性能对于用户参与度和留存率至关重要。为了让服务更贴近用户，CDN 在全球数百个位置部署了服务器。这些服务器位置被称为“接入点”（Point of Presence，简称 POP）。POP 内的服务器现在通常被称为“边缘服务器”（Edge Server）。在全球拥有大量 POP，可确保每位用户都能连接到附近的高速边缘服务器。不同的 CDN 采用不同的技术将用户请求路由到最近的 POP。常见的技术包括基于 DNS 的路由和 Anycast。在基于 DNS 的路由中，每个 POP 都有自己独立的 IP 地址。当用户查询 CDN 的 IP 地址时，DNS 会返回距离用户最近的 POP 节点的 IP 地址。在任播（any cast）机制下，所有 POP 节点共享同一个 IP 地址。当请求进入任播网络并针对该 IP 地址发起时，网络会将请求发送到距离该请求最近的 POP 节点。每个边缘服务器都充当反向代理，并配备庞大的内容缓存。我们在之前的视频中讨论过反向代理，如需了解更多，请查看描述内容。在边缘服务器的内容缓存中设置内容缓存。如果某部分内容已存在于缓存中，即可快速返回给用户。由于边缘服务器仅需从源服务器获取静态内容的副本，因此该内容不在缓存中。这大大降低了源服务器集群的延迟和带宽需求。现代 CDN 还可以将静态内容转换为更优化的格式。例如，你可以即时修改 JavaScript 打包文件，或将图片文件从旧格式转换为 WebP 或 AVIF 等现代格式。S-Server 在现代 XDDP 栈中也扮演着非常重要的角色，即 TLS 连接在边缘服务器终止。TLS 握手过程成本较高。该公司使用的 TLS 版本（如 TLS 1.2）需要多次网络往返才能建立连接。通过在边缘终止 TLS 连接，可以显著减少用户建立加密 TCP 连接时的延迟。这也是许多现代应用即使对于动态且可缓存的 HTTP 内容，也选择通过 CDN 发送的原因之一。除了性能优势，现代 CDN 还带来另外两大主要好处，首先是安全性。所有现代 CDN 在边缘都拥有巨大的网络容量，这是有效抵御大规模 D-DOS 攻击的关键。由于其网络容量远超攻击者，因此特别有效，尤其是当 CDN 建立在任播（any-cast）网络上时。这使得 CDN 能够将攻击流量分散到大量服务器上。其次，现代 CDN 能够提高可用性。CDN 本质上高度分布式，通过在多个 POP 点提供内容副本，CDN 能够比源服务器承受更多的硬件故障。现代 CDN 提供了诸多优势。在处理 HTTP 流量时，我们应当使用 CDN。如果您想了解更多关于系统设计的内容，欢迎查看我们的书籍和免费每周通讯。如果您学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P25 What is RPC_ gRPC Introduction

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=25)　时长 6分10秒

**原文**

What is GRPC? When should we use it? Let's take a look. GRPC is an open source we mode procedure call framework created by Google in 2016. It was a rewrite of their internal RPC infrastructure that they used for years. But first, what is an RPC or a remote procedure call? A local procedure call is a function call within a process to execute some code. A remote procedure call enables one machine to invoke some code on another machine as if it is local function call from a user's perspective. A GRPC is a popular implementation of RPC. Many organizations have adopted GRPC as to prefer RPC mechanism to connect a large number of microservices running within and across data centers. What makes GRPC so popular? Let's dive a little deeper. First, GRPC has a thriving developer ecosystem. It makes it very easy to develop production quality and type-saving APIs that scale well. The core of this ecosystem is to use a protocol buffers as its data in the change format. Protocol buffers is a language in Nostick and platform in Nostick mechanism for encoding structured data. GRPC uses protocol buffers to encode and send data over the wires by default. While GRPC can support other encoding formats like JSON, Protocol buffers provide several advantages that makes it the encoding format of choice for GRPC. Protocol buffers support strongly type schema definition. The structure of the data over the wire is defined in a protocol file. Protocol buffers provide broad tooling support to turn the schema defined the protocol into data access classes for all popular programming languages. A GRPC service is also defined in a protocol by specifying RPC method parameters and return types. The same tooling is used to generate GRPC client and server code from the protocol. Developers use these generated classes in the client to make RPC calls and in the server to fulfill the RPC requests. By supporting many programming languages, the client and server can independently choose the programming language and ecosystem best suited for their own particular use cases. This is traditionally not the case for most other RPC frameworks. The second reason why GRPC is so popular is because it is high performance out of the box. Two factors contributes to its performance. First, is the protocol buffer is very efficient binary encoding format. It is much faster than JSON. Second, GRPC is built on top of HTTP2 to provide high performance foundation as scale. The use of HTTP2 brings many benefits. We discussed HTTP2 in an earlier video. Check out the link in the description for information. GRPC uses HTTP2 streams. It allows multiple streams of messages over a single long lift TCP connection. This allows the GRPC framework to handle many concurrent RPC calls over a small number of TCP connections between clients and servers. To understand how GRPC works, let's walk through a typical flow from a GRPC client to a GRPC server. In this example, the order service is the GRPC client and the payment service is the GRPC server. When order service makes a GRPC call to the payment service, it invokes the client code generated by GRPC tooling at built time. This generated client code is called a client stop. GRPC and codes the data pass to the client stop in the protocol buffers and sends it to the low-level transport layer. GRPC sends the data over the network as a stream of HTTP2 data frames. Because of binary encoding and network optimization, GRPC is said to be five times faster than JSON. The payment service receives the packets from the network decodes them and invokes the server application. The result returned from the server application gets encoded into protocol buffers and sends to the transport layer. The order service receives the packets decodes them and sends the result to the client application. As we see from the example above, GRPC is very easy to implement. Now it is so easy. Why do we not see widespread use of GRPC between web clients and web servers? One reason is that GRPC relies on lower-level access to HTTP2 primitives. No browsers currently provide the level of control required over web requests to support a GRPC client. It is possible to make GRPC calls from the browser with the help of a proxy. This technology is called GRPC web. However, the feature set is not fully compatible with GRPC and is usage remains low compared to GRPC itself. So where does GRPC shine and when should we use it? GRPC is the inner service communication mechanism of choice between microservices in the data centers. It's brought support for many programming languages allow services to choose their own languages and developer ecosystems best suited for their own use cases. We also see increasing use of GRPC in the native mobile clients. It's efficiency and performance. Mix a lot of sense in energy and bandwidth-constring environments that are mobile devices. You would like to learn more about system design. Check out our books and free weekly newsletter. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

什么是 gRPC？我们何时应该使用它？让我们一起来看看。gRPC 是 Google 在 2016 年创建的一个开源远程过程调用框架。它是对 Google 多年来内部使用的 RPC 基础设施的重写。但首先，什么是 RPC 或远程过程调用？本地过程调用是指进程内部为了执行某些代码而进行的函数调用。而远程过程调用则允许一台机器调用另一台机器上的代码，从用户的角度来看，这就像调用本地函数一样。gRPC 是 RPC 的一种流行实现。许多组织都采用了 gRPC，因为它比传统的 RPC 机制更适合连接在数据中心内部和跨数据中心运行的大量微服务。是什么让 gRPC 如此受欢迎？让我们深入了解一下。首先，gRPC 拥有蓬勃发展的开发者生态系统。它让开发生产级、类型安全且具备良好扩展性的 API 变得非常容易。该生态系统的核心是使用 Protocol Buffers 作为其数据交换格式。Protocol Buffers 是一种语言无关、平台无关的结构化数据编码机制。默认情况下，gRPC 使用 Protocol Buffers 来编码并通过网络传输数据。虽然 GRPC 可以支持 JSON 等其他编码格式，但 Protocol buffers 具备多项优势，使其成为 GRPC 的首选编码格式。Protocol buffers 支持强类型模式定义。传输过程中的数据结构在协议文件中定义。Protocol buffers 提供广泛的工具支持，可将协议中定义的模式转换为适用于所有主流编程语言的访问类。GRPC 服务也通过协议进行定义，具体方式是指定 RPC 方法的参数和返回类型。相同的工具可用于从协议生成 GRPC 客户端和服务器代码。开发者在客户端使用这些生成的类发起 RPC 调用，在服务器端则用于处理 RPC 请求。由于支持多种编程语言，客户端和服务器可以独立选择最适合其特定用例的编程语言和生态系统。这在传统上并非大多数其他 RPC 框架所能实现的优势。GRPC 广受欢迎的第二个原因是其开箱即用的高性能特性。有两个因素促成了其卓越性能。首先，Protocol buffer 是一种非常高效的二进制编码格式。它的速度远快于 JSON。其次，GRPC 构建在 HTTP2 之上，为规模化扩展提供了高性能基础。使用 HTTP2 带来了诸多优势。我们在之前的视频中讨论过 HTTP2，请查看描述中的链接以获取相关信息。GRPC 使用 HTTP2 流，允许多个消息流在单个长生命周期 TCP 连接上传输。这使得 GRPC 框架能够通过少量 TCP 连接，在客户端和服务器之间处理大量并发的 RPC 调用。为了理解 GRPC 的工作原理，让我们逐步了解从 GRPC 客户端到 GRPC 服务器的典型流程。在本例中，订单服务是 GRPC 客户端，支付服务是 GRPC 服务器。当订单服务向支付服务发起 GRPC 调用时，它会调用由 GRPC 工具在构建时生成的客户端代码。这段生成的客户端代码被称为客户端桩（client stub）。GRPC 将传递给客户端桩的数据编码为协议缓冲区（protocol buffers），然后将其发送到底层传输层。GRPC 以 HTTP2 数据帧流的形式通过网络发送数据。由于采用了二进制编码和网络优化，GRPC 的速度据称比 JSON 快五倍。支付服务从网络接收数据包，对其进行解码，并调用服务器应用程序。服务器应用程序返回的结果会被编码为 Protocol Buffers，然后发送到传输层。订单服务接收数据包，对其进行解码，并将结果发送给客户端应用程序。从上面的例子中我们可以看到，GRPC 的实现非常简单。现在它如此容易，那为什么我们没有在 Web 客户端和 Web 服务器之间广泛看到 GRPC 的使用呢？一个原因是 GRPC 依赖于对 HTTP2 原语的底层访问。目前没有浏览器提供对 Web 请求所需的控制级别来支持 GRPC 客户端。借助代理，可以从浏览器发起 GRPC 调用。这项技术被称为 GRPC Web。然而，其功能集与 GRPC 并不完全兼容，与 GRPC 本身相比，其使用率仍然较低。那么，GRPC 的优势体现在哪里？我们应该在什么时候使用它呢？GRPC 是数据中心内微服务之间首选的内部服务通信机制。它支持多种编程语言，使服务能够选择最适合其自身用例的语言和开发者生态系统。我们还看到，原生移动客户端越来越多地使用 GRPC。它的效率和性能在移动设备这类能源和带宽受限的环境中非常契合。如果您想进一步了解系统设计，欢迎查看我们的书籍和免费每周通讯。如果您学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P26 SSL, TLS, HTTPS Explained

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=26)　时长 5分54秒

**原文**

What is so important about HTTPS that these days, most websites require it? How does HTTPS work? We'll answer these questions in this video. Let's dive right in. With the HTTPS communication between the browser and the server is in plain text, this means that the password you enter or the credit card number you send over in and that can be read by anyone who has the ability to intercept it. HTTPS is designed to solve this problem, to make the data send over internet unreadable by anyone other than the sender and the receiver. HTTPS is an extension of the HTTP protocol, we discussed HTTP in an earlier video. Check the description it will like to learn more about it. With HTTPS, data is sending in an encrypted form using something called TLS. TLS stands for transport layer security. If the encrypted data gets intercepted by a hacker, all they could see is jumbo data. Let's take a look at how the TLS handshake works. They are several steps. Step 1. Just like in the case for HTTP, the browser establishes a TCP connection with the server. Step 2. This is where the TLS handshake begins. The browser sends a client hollow to the server. In this hollow message, the browser tells the server the following things. 1. What TLS version it can support, it could be TLS 1.2, TLS 1.3, etc. 2. What cyber suites is supports, the cyber suite is a set of encryption algorithms to use and crypt data. After receiving the client hollow, the server gets to choose the cyber suite and the TLS version to use based on the options it got from the client. It sends those in a server hollow message back to the client. The server then sends the certificate to the client. The certificate includes a lot of different things. One of the key things is the public plea for the server. The client uses the public key in something called a symmetric encryption. In a symmetric encryption, a piece of data that is encrypted by a public key can only be decrypted by the private key. We will discuss how this is used in a bit. This concludes step 2. The hollow phase of the TLS handshake. At this point, the client has a service certificate and the client and server have agreed on the TLS version and the service we use. Now in the step 3, this is the step where the client and the server come up with a share encryption key to use to encrypt data. This is where the symmetric encryption comes into the picture. Again with a symmetric encryption, the data encrypted on the client side using the public key from the server can only be decrypted by the server. This is how the client sends an encryption key safely to the server over the wide open internet. All this is done in the client key exchange message. The exact detail varies depending on the service we used. Here we use RSA as an example since it is the easiest to understand. With RSA, the client generates an encryption key, also call a session key and encrypts it with the server public key and sends a encrypted session key to the server overlay in the internet. The server receives the encrypted session key and decrypts it with this private key. Now both sides hold the session key and this is where they enter step 4 of the TLS handshake. Where they use the session key and agree upon server suite to send and encrypt the data back and forth in a secure, bi-directional channel. Now you may ask, why don't we just use a symmetric encryption for everything? Why switch to symmetric encryption at all? The main reason is that asymmetric encryption is computationally expensive. It is not really suitable for bulk data transmission. Before we close, there are two finer points I would like to discuss. First, the handshake we talked about applied to TLS 1.2 while the latest version is TLS 1.3 and TLS 1.3 is supported on all major browsers. As we can see in our illustration, TLS 1.2 takes two network ramp trips to complete. This is one of the major improvements of TLS 1.3. It optimizes the handshake to reduce the number of network ramp trips to one. We decided to talk about TLS 1.2 because we reviewed TLS 1.3 as an optimization. As with most optimizations, it is a bit harder to explain. That's why we chose TLS 1.2 instead. The core concepts in TLS 1.2 still applies to TLS 1.3. The second finer point we like to discuss is that in the explanation about we use TLS 8 for asymmetric encryption to securely exchange the asymmetric session key. Again, we chose TLS 8 because it is easy to understand. However, asymmetric encryption is not the only way to share the session key between the client and the server. In fact, in TLS 1.3, TLS 1.3, TLS 8 no longer supported as a method for key exchange. If we helmet is a more common way nowadays for exchanging session keys. If we helmet is complicated, but in the nutshell, it uses some advanced math involving large prime numbers to derive a shared session key without ever transmitting a public key over the network. This is it for XTDPS. You would like to learn more about system design, check out our books and free weekly newsletter. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

HTTPS 为什么如此重要，以至于如今大多数网站都要求使用它？HTTPS 是如何工作的？我们将在本视频中解答这些问题。让我们直接开始吧。当浏览器与服务器之间的通信是明文形式时，这意味着您输入的密码或发送的信用卡号，任何能够拦截数据的人都可以读取。HTTPS 的设计正是为了解决这个问题，确保通过互联网发送的数据除了发送者和接收者之外，任何人都无法读取。HTTPS 是 HTTP 协议的扩展，我们在之前的视频中讨论过 HTTP。请查看描述部分，那里有相关链接，可了解更多相关信息。使用 HTTPS 时，数据会通过一种称为 TLS 的技术以加密形式发送。TLS 全称是传输层安全（Transport Layer Security）。如果加密数据被黑客拦截，他们看到的将只是一堆乱码。让我们来看看 TLS 握手是如何工作的。整个过程包含几个步骤。步骤 1：与 HTTP 的情况一样，浏览器首先与服务器建立 TCP 连接。步骤 2：TLS 握手从这里开始。浏览器会向服务器发送一个客户端问候（client hello）。在这条客户端消息中，浏览器会向服务器传达以下信息：1. 浏览器支持的 TLS 版本，例如 TLS 1.2、TLS 1.3 等；2. 浏览器支持的加密套件。加密套件是一组用于加密和解密数据的加密算法。收到客户端消息后，服务器会根据客户端提供的选项，选择要使用的加密套件和 TLS 版本，并将这些选择通过服务器消息返回给客户端。随后，服务器会将证书发送给客户端。证书包含许多不同的信息，其中关键的一项就是服务器的公钥。客户端会在一种称为对称加密的过程中使用此公钥。在对称加密中，用公钥加密的数据只能由对应的私钥解密。我们稍后将讨论其具体应用方式。至此，TLS 握手协议的客户端阶段（第 2 步）结束。此时，客户端已收到服务器证书，且客户端与服务器已就 TLS 版本和加密套件达成一致。接下来是第 3 步，客户端和服务器将共同生成一个共享的加密密钥，用于后续数据的加密。这就是对称加密发挥作用的地方。同样，在使用对称加密时，客户端使用服务器的公钥在客户端一侧加密的数据，只能由服务器进行解密。客户端正是通过这种方式，在开放的互联网上安全地将加密密钥发送给服务器。所有这些操作都是在客户端密钥交换消息中完成的。具体细节因所使用的服务而异。这里我们以 RSA 为例，因为它最易于理解。使用 RSA 时，客户端会生成一个加密密钥（也称为会话密钥），并使用服务器的公钥对其进行加密，然后将加密后的会话密钥通过网络发送给服务器。服务器收到加密后的会话密钥后，使用其私钥进行解密。现在，双方都拥有了会话密钥，这时他们便进入 TLS 握手的第 4 步。在该步骤中，他们使用该会话密钥并协商好加密套件，以便通过安全的、双向的通道来回传输和加密数据。现在你可能会问，为什么我们不直接使用对称加密来处理一切呢？为什么还要切换到对称加密呢？主要原因在于非对称加密的计算开销较大。它并不真正适合批量数据传输。在结束之前，还有两个细节问题我想讨论一下。首先，我们之前讨论的握手协议适用于 TLS 1.2，而最新版本是 TLS 1.3，并且 TLS 1.3 已得到所有主流浏览器的支持。从我们的示意图中可以看到，TLS 1.2 需要往返两次网络传输才能完成握手。这也是 TLS 1.3 的主要改进之一：它优化了握手过程，将网络传输次数减少到一次。我们之所以选择讲解 TLS 1.2，是因为我们将 TLS 1.3 视为一种优化方案。和大多数优化一样，它的解释起来稍微复杂一些，因此我们最终选择了 TLS 1.2。不过，TLS 1.2 中的核心概念仍然适用于 TLS 1.3。第二个细节是，在之前的讲解中，我们使用 TLS 8 进行非对称加密，以安全地交换非对称会话密钥。同样，我们选择 TLS 8 是因为它更易于理解。然而，非对称加密并非客户端与服务器之间共享会话密钥的唯一方式。实际上，在 TLS 1.3 中，TLS 8 已不再被支持为密钥交换的方法。如今，更常见的密钥交换方式是 Diffie-Hellman 密钥交换。如果我们的头盔很复杂，但简而言之，它使用了一些涉及大素数的先进数学方法来推导出一个共享的会话密钥，而无需在网络上传输公钥。以上就是 XTDPS 的全部内容。如果你想了解更多关于系统设计的内容，可以看看我们的书籍和免费的每周简报。如果你学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P27 Process vs Thread _ Popular Interview Question

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=27)　时长 3分51秒

**原文**

What's the difference between process and thread? This is a common interview question. Let's take a look. To better answer this question, let's first understand what a program is. A program is an executable file. It contains the code or a set of processing instructions that is stored as a file on disk. When the code in the program is loaded into memory and executed by the processor, it becomes a process. An active process also includes the resources the program needs to run. These resources are managed by the operating system. Some examples are processor registers, program counters, stack pointers, memory pages assigned to the process for its heap and stack, et cetera. There is an important property of a process that is worth mentioning. Each process is its own memory address space. One process cannot corrupt the memory space of another process. This means that when one process malfunctions, other processes keep running. From its famous for ticking advantage of this process isolation by running each tab in its own process, when one tab misbehaves due to a bug or malicious attack, other tabs are unaffected. Now, what is the thread? A thread is the unit of execution within the process. A process has at least one thread. It is called a main thread. It is not uncommon for a process to have many threads. Each thread has its own stack. Earlier we mentioned registers, program counters, and stack pointers as being part of a process. It is more accurate to say that those things belong to a thread. Threads within a process-share, a memory address space. It is possible to communicate between threads using that share memory space. However, one misbehaving thread could bring down the entire process. How does the operating system run the thread or process on the CPU? This is handled by context-witching. During a context-switch, one process is switch-out of the CPU, so another process can run. The operating system stores the states of the current running process, so the process can be restored and resumed execution at a later point. It then resorts the previously saved states of a different process and resumed execution for that process. The context-witching is expensive. It involves saving and loading of registers, switching our memory pages and updating various kernel data structures. Switching execution between threads also requires context-witching. This generally faster the switch contacts between threads than between processes. There are fewer states to track and more importantly, since threads share the same memory address space, there is no need to switch our virtual memory pages, which is one of the more expensive operation during a context-witch. Context-witching is so costly, there are other mechanisms to try to minimize it, some examples of fibers and color teens. These mechanisms trace complexity for even lower context-witching cost. In general, they are cooperatively scheduled, that is, they must you control for others to run. In other words, the application is self-handles task scheduling. It is a responsibility of the application to make sure a long-running task is broken up by using periodically. This concludes our discussion of process and threads. If you would like to learn more about system design, check out our books and weekly newsletter. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

进程和线程之间有什么区别？这是一个常见的面试问题。我们一起来看看。为了更好地回答这个问题，我们首先需要了解什么是程序。程序是一个可执行文件，它包含代码或一组处理指令，并以文件形式存储在磁盘上。当程序中的代码被加载到内存并由处理器执行时，它就变成了一个进程。一个正在运行的进程还包括程序运行所需的资源。这些资源由操作系统管理，例如处理器寄存器、程序计数器、栈指针、分配给进程堆和栈的内存页等。进程有一个重要的特性值得提及：每个进程都有自己独立的内存地址空间。一个进程不能破坏另一个进程的内存空间。这意味着当一个进程发生故障时，其他进程仍能继续运行。Chrome 浏览器就著名地利用了这种进程隔离的优势，它为每个标签页都分配一个独立的进程。当某个标签页因错误或恶意攻击而异常时，其他标签页不会受到影响。那么，什么是线程呢？线程是进程内的执行单元。一个进程至少包含一个线程，这个线程被称为主线程。一个进程拥有多个线程的情况并不少见。每个线程都有自己独立的栈。之前我们提到过寄存器、程序计数器和栈指针都属于进程的一部分。更准确地说，这些元素实际上属于线程。同一进程内的线程共享内存地址空间，因此可以通过共享的内存空间进行通信。然而，一个行为异常的线程可能会导致整个进程崩溃。操作系统如何在 CPU 上运行线程或进程呢？这是通过上下文切换来完成的。在上下文切换期间，当前运行的进程会被切换出 CPU，以便另一个进程能够运行。操作系统会保存当前运行进程的状态，以便之后可以恢复该进程并继续执行。接着，系统会恢复另一个进程之前保存的状态，并继续执行该进程。上下文切换的开销较大，它涉及寄存器的保存与加载、内存页面的切换以及多种内核数据结构的更新。在线程之间切换执行也需要进行上下文切换。通常，线程之间的上下文切换比进程之间的切换更快。这是因为需要跟踪的状态更少，更重要的是，由于线程共享相同的内存地址空间，因此无需切换虚拟内存页——而这正是上下文切换过程中最昂贵的操作之一。由于上下文切换的成本非常高，因此存在一些其他机制来尽量减少这种开销，例如纤程（fibers）和协程（coroutines）。这些机制通过增加复杂性来进一步降低上下文切换的成本。通常，它们采用协作式调度，也就是说，必须由程序员显式控制它们的运行。换句话说，应用程序需要自行处理任务调度。应用程序有责任确保耗时任务能被定期中断，从而被拆分执行。以上就是关于进程和线程的讨论。如果您想了解更多关于系统设计的内容，欢迎查看我们的书籍和每周通讯。如果您学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P28 What is OSI Model _ Real World Examples

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=28)　时长 4分45秒

**原文**

How does data send over the internet? What does that have to do with the OSI model? How does TCP-IP fit into this? Let's take a look. The OSI model or the open systems interconnect model is a theoretical framework that provides one way of thinking about networking. It splits the network communication between two devices on a network into seven abstraction layers. The physical layer is the bottom most layer. It is responsible for transmitting raw bits of data across a physical connection. The data link layer is the second layer. It takes the raw bits from the physical layer and organizes them into frames. It ensures that the frames are delivered to the correct destination. To Ethernet, primarily lives in this layer. The network layer is the third layer. This is responsible for routing data frames across different networks. The IP part of TCP-IP is a well-known example of this layer. The transport layer is the fourth layer. Your handles end to end communication between two nodes. This is the layer where TCP and UDP live. TCP provides reliable end to end communication between devices. It does this by dividing the data into small, manageable segments and sending each segment individually. Each segment has a sequence number attached to it. The receiving end uses the sequence numbers to reassemble the data in the correct order. TCP also provides error checking to make sure that the data was not corrupted during transmission. UDP is another popular protocol in the transport layer. It is similar to TCP but it is simpler and faster. Unlike TCP, UDP does not provide the same level of error checking or reliability. It simply sends packets of data from one device to another. The receiving end is responsible for determining whether the packets will receive correctly. If an error is detected, the receiving end simply describes the packet. The remaining layers are session, presentation, and application layers. This is where the OSI model loses its usefulness in practice. They are two fine-grained and do not really reflect reality. In general, it is sufficient to collapse them into a single layer and consider application protocols like HTTP as simply layer 7 protocols. Now let's go through an example and examine how data moves through the layers when transmitting over the network. When a user sends an HTTP request to a web server over the network, the HTTP header is added to the data at the application layer. Then, HTTP header is added to the data. It is encapsulated into TCP segments at the transport layer. The header contains the source port, destination port and sequence number. The segments are then encapsulated with an IP header at the network layer. The IP header contains the source and destination IP addresses. A Mac header is added to the data link layer with the source and destination Mac addresses. It is worth noting that in the real world, this is a bit nuanced. The Mac addresses are usually not the Mac address of the sending and the receiving ends. They are the Mac addresses of the routing devices in the next hop of a usually long journey across the internet. They encapsulate the frames of send over the network in raw bits in the physical layer. When a web server receives the raw bits from the network, it reverses the process. The headers that we moved layer by layer, and eventually the web server processes to HTTP request. To conclude, the OSI mod is one way of thinking about networks. Its primary purpose is educational. Even though the layers don't fit the real world use cases perfectly, they are still widely used by networking vendors and cloud providers as a short-hand to describe where the networking products sit in the OSI model. For example, cloud load balancers are broadly divided into two categories, L4 and L7. An L7 load balancer is a short-hand to mean that the load balancer operates at the application protocol layer like HTTP or HTTPS. An L4 load balancer on the other hand operates at the TCP level. You would like to learn more about system design, check out our books and weekly newsletter. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

数据是如何通过互联网发送的？这与 OSI 模型有什么关系？TCP-IP 又如何融入其中呢？我们一起来看看。OSI 模型，即开放系统互连模型，是一种用于理解网络通信的理论框架。它将网络中两个设备之间的通信划分为七个抽象层次。物理层是最低的一层，负责通过物理连接传输原始数据位。数据链路层是第二层，它从物理层接收原始数据位，并将其组织成帧，同时确保这些帧能够被传送到正确的目的地。以太网主要就工作在这一层。网络层是第三层，负责将数据帧路由到不同的网络。TCP-IP 中的 IP 部分就是这一层的一个典型例子。传输层是第四层，负责两个节点之间的端到端通信。TCP 和 UDP 就属于这一层。TCP 提供了设备之间可靠的端到端通信。它通过将数据划分为多个易于管理的小段，并逐个发送这些分段来实现这一功能。每个分段都附带一个序列号。接收端利用这些序列号将数据重新组装成正确的顺序。TCP 还提供错误检查功能，以确保数据在传输过程中未被损坏。UDP 是传输层中另一种常用的协议。它与 TCP 类似，但更简单、速度更快。与 TCP 不同，UDP 不提供同等程度的错误检查或可靠性。它只是将数据包从一个设备发送到另一个设备。接收端负责判断数据包是否被正确接收。如果检测到错误，接收端会直接丢弃该数据包。其余的层是会话层、表示层和应用层。在这些层上，OSI 模型在实践中失去了其实用性。它们划分得过于细致，并未真正反映现实情况。通常，将它们合并为单一层，并将 HTTP 等应用协议视为简单的第 7 层协议就足够了。现在让我们通过一个示例来了解数据在网络传输过程中如何逐层传递。当用户通过网络向 Web 服务器发送 HTTP 请求时，HTTP 标头会在应用层被添加到数据中。然后，HTTP 标头被添加到数据中。在传输层，数据被封装为 TCP 段。该标头包含源端口、目标端口和序列号。接着，在网络层，这些段被封装上 IP 标头。IP 标头包含源 IP 地址和目标 IP 地址。在数据链路层，数据会被添加一个 MAC 标头，其中包含源 MAC 地址和目标 MAC 地址。值得注意的是，在现实世界中，情况会稍微复杂一些。这些 MAC 地址通常并非发送端和接收端的 MAC 地址，而是互联网上一段漫长旅程中下一跳路由设备的 MAC 地址。在物理层，这些数据帧被封装为原始比特在网络中传输。当 Web 服务器从网络中接收到这些原始比特后，便会逆向执行整个过程，逐层剥离我们之前添加的各个标头，最终处理出 HTTP 请求。总而言之，OSI 模型是思考网络的一种方式。其主要目的是用于教学。尽管这些层次并不能完美对应现实世界中的用例，但它们仍被网络厂商和云提供商广泛用作一种简化的说法，用以描述其网络产品在 OSI 模型中所处的位置。例如，云负载均衡器通常分为两类：L4 和 L7。L7 负载均衡器是“负载均衡器在应用层协议（如 HTTP 或 HTTPS）上运行”的简写。而 L4 负载均衡器则在 TCP 层面运行。如果您想进一步了解系统设计，欢迎查看我们的书籍和每周简报。如果您学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P29 CAP Theorem Simplified _ System Design Fundamentals

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=29)　时长 5分33秒

**原文**

What is cap theorem? How useful is it to system design? Let's take a look. The cap theorem is a concept in computer science that explains the trade-offs between consistency, availability, and partition tolerance in distributed systems. Consistency refers to the property of a system where all nodes have a consistent view of the data. It means all clients see the same data at the same time, no matter which note they connect to. Availability refers to the ability of a system to respond to requests from users at all times. Partition tolerance refers to the ability of a system to continue operating even if there is a network partition. But what is a network partition? A network partition happens when nodes in a distributed system are enabled to communicate with each other due to network failures. When there is a network partition, a system has choose between consistency and availability. If the system prioritizes consistency, it may become unavailable until the partition is resolved. If the system prioritizes availability, it may allow updates to the data. This could result in data in consistencies until the partition is resolved. Now let's go through a concrete example. Let's say with a tiny bang with two ATM's connected over the network. The ATM supports three operations, deposit, withdraw, and check balance. No matter what happens, the balance should never go below zero. There is no central database in this bang to keep the account balance. It is stored on both ATM's. When a customer uses an ATM, the balance is updated on both ATM's over the network. This ensures that the ATM's have a consistent view of their account balance. If there is a network partition and the ATM's are enabled to communicate with each other, the system has choose between consistency and an availability. If the bang prioritizes consistency, the ATM may refuse to process deposits of withdraws until the partition is resolved. This ensures that the balance remains consistent, but the system is unavailable to customers. If the bang prioritizes availability, the ATM may allow deposits and withdraws to occur, but the balance may become inconsistent until the partition is resolved. When there is a network partition, the customer could withdraw the entire balance from both ATM's. When the network comes back online, the inconsistencies is resolved and now the balance is negative. That is not good. Now let's go through another example and see how a social media platform could apply the kept theorem. During a network partition, if two users are commenting on the same pose at the same time, one users comment may not be visible to the other users until the partition is resolved. Alternatively, if the platform prioritizes consistency, the commenting feature may be unavailable to users until the partition is resolved. For a social network, it is often acceptable to prioritize availability at the cost of users seeing slightly different views some of the time. The kept theorem may sound very simple, but the real world is messy. As with many things in software engineering, this is all about trade-offs and the choices are not always so black and white. The kept theorems assumes 100% availability or 100% consistency. In the real world, there are degrees of consistency and availability. That distributed system designers must carefully consider. This is where the simplistic model of the kept theorem could be misleading. Now back to the bank example. During the a network partition, the ATM could allow only balance inquiries to be processed while it deposits and withdraws a blocked. Alternatively, the bank could implement a hybrid approach. For example, the ATM could allow balance inquiries and small withdraws to be processed during a partition, but block large withdraws and all deposits until the partition is resolved. This worth noting that in the real world, reconciliation after a network partition could get very messy. The bank example above is simple to reconcile. In real life, the data structures involved could be complex and challenging to reconcile. A good example of a complex data structure is Google Docs, resolving conflicting updates could be tricky. So is the kept theorem useful? Yes, it is a useful tool to help us things through the high-level trade-offs to consider when there is a network partition. This is a good starting point, but it does not provide a complete picture of the trade-offs to consider when designing a well-rounded distributed system. Specifically, when the system is operating normally without a network failure, which is most of the time, there is an entire set of interesting trade-offs to consider between latency and consistency. This is covered by the pack-held theorem, which we should cover in another video. You would like to learn more about system design, check out our books and weekly newsletter. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

什么是 CAP 定理？它在系统设计中有多大用处？我们一起来看看。CAP 定理是计算机科学中的一个概念，用于解释分布式系统中一致性、可用性和分区容忍度之间的权衡。一致性是指系统中所有节点对数据都有一致的视图。这意味着无论客户端连接到哪个节点，都能看到相同的数据。可用性是指系统随时响应用户请求的能力。分区容忍度是指系统在发生网络分区时仍能继续运行的能力。但什么是网络分区呢？网络分区是指由于网络故障，分布式系统中的节点之间无法正常通信。当发生网络分区时，系统必须在一致性和可用性之间做出选择。如果系统优先考虑一致性，那么在分区解决之前，系统可能会变得不可用。如果系统优先考虑可用性，则可能允许对数据进行更新，这可能导致在分区解决之前出现数据不一致的情况。现在，我们通过一个具体例子来进一步说明。假设在一个小村庄里，有两台 ATM 通过网络连接。这些 ATM 支持三种操作：存款、取款和查询余额。无论发生什么情况，账户余额绝不能低于零。这个村庄没有中央数据库来存储账户余额，余额信息保存在每台 ATM 上。当客户使用 ATM 时，余额会通过网络在两台 ATM 上同时更新。这确保了两台 ATM 对账户余额有一致的看法。如果出现网络分区，并且 ATM 之间仍然能够相互通信，系统必须在一致性和可用性之间做出选择。如果村庄优先考虑一致性，ATM 可能会拒绝处理存款或取款操作，直到网络分区问题解决。这虽然能确保余额的一致性，但系统对客户来说就不可用了。如果村庄优先考虑可用性，ATM 可能会允许进行存款和取款操作，但余额在分区问题解决前可能会变得不一致。在网络分区期间，客户可以从两台 ATM 中取出全部余额。当网络恢复后，不一致的情况得到解决，此时账户余额可能为负数。那不太好。现在让我们来看另一个例子，看看社交媒体平台如何应用 CAP 定理。在网络分区期间，如果两个用户同时评论同一个帖子，其中一个用户的评论可能在分区解决前对另一个用户不可见。或者，如果平台优先考虑一致性，那么在分区解决前，评论功能可能对用户不可用。对于社交网络来说，通常可以接受牺牲一致性来换取可用性，即使这意味着用户有时可能会看到略有不同的内容。CAP 定理听起来可能非常简单，但现实世界是复杂的。正如软件工程中的许多事情一样，这完全关乎权衡，而选择并不总是非黑即白。CAP 定理假设的是 100% 的可用性或 100% 的一致性。而在现实世界中，一致性和可用性都存在不同程度的权衡。分布式系统设计师必须仔细考虑这一点。这正是 CAP 定理的简化模型可能产生误导的地方。现在回到银行示例。在网络分区期间，ATM 可以只允许处理余额查询，同时暂停存取款操作。或者，银行也可以采用混合方法。例如，在分区期间，ATM 可以允许处理余额查询和小额取款，但阻止大额取款以及所有存款操作，直到分区问题解决。值得注意的是，在现实世界中，网络分区后的数据对账可能会变得非常复杂。上述银行的例子相对容易对账。而在实际生活中，涉及的数据结构可能非常复杂，对账难度也很大。Google Docs 就是一个复杂数据结构的典型例子，解决冲突的更新可能非常棘手。那么，CAP 定理是否有用呢？是的，它是一个很有用的工具，能帮助我们理清在网络分区情况下需要考虑的高层权衡。这是一个很好的起点，但它并未完整呈现设计一个完善的分布式系统时所需要考虑的全部权衡因素。具体来说，当系统在没有网络故障的情况下正常运行时——这种情况占大多数——在延迟和一致性之间有一整套值得考虑的权衡取舍。这由“pack-held 定理”来涵盖，我们将在另一个视频中详细讲解。如果你想了解更多关于系统设计的内容，可以看看我们的书籍和每周通讯。如果你学到了新知识，欢迎订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P30 Kubernetes Explained in 6 Minutes _ k8s Architecture

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=30)　时长 6分29秒

**原文**

Why this Kubernetes? Why is it called KAS? What makes it so popular? Let's take a look. Kubernetes is an open source container orchestration platform. It all amazed the deployment, scaling, and management of containerized applications. Kubernetes can be traced back to Google's internal container orchestration system board, which managed the deployment of thousands of applications within Google. In 2014, Google opens source a version of work that is Kubernetes. Now, why is it called KAS? This is a somewhat nerdy way of abbreviating long words. The number 8 in KAS referred to the 8 letters between the first letter K and the last letter S in the work Kubernetes. Other examples that I18 and for internationalization and L10 and for localization. A Kubernetes cluster is a set of machines called notes that are used to run containerized applications. There are two core pieces in a Kubernetes cluster. The first is the control plane. This responsible for managing the state of the cluster. In production environments, the control plane usually runs on multiple notes that span across several data centers zones. The second is the set of worker notes. These notes run the containerized application workloads. The containerized applications run in a pot. Pot are the smallest deployable units in Kubernetes. Pot hosts one or more containers and provides share storage and networking for those containers. Pot's are created and managed by Kubernetes control plane. They are the basic building blocks of Kubernetes applications. Now, let's dive a bit deeper into the control plane. You consist of a number of core components. They are the API server, SCD, scheduler, and the controller manager. The API server is the primary interface between the control plane and the rest of the cluster. It exposes a restful API that allows clients to interact with the control plane and submit requests to manage the cluster. SCD is a distributed key value store. It stores the clusters for system state. This used by the API server and other components of the control plane to store and retrieve information about the cluster. The scheduler is responsible for scheduling parts onto the worker notes in the cluster. It uses information about the resources we acquire by the parts and available resources on the worker notes to make placement decisions. The controller manager is responsible for running controllers that manage the state of the cluster. Some examples include a replication controller, which ensures that the desired number of replicas of a part running and the deployment controller, which manages the rolling updates and rollbacks of deployments. Next, let's dive deeper into the worker notes. The core components of Kubernetes that run on the worker notes include Kubernetes, container runtime, and coop proxy. The Kubernetes is a demand that runs on each worker note. This responsible for communicating with the control plane. It receives instruction from the control plane about which parts to run on the note and ensures that the desired state of the parts is maintained. The container runtime runs the containers on the note. It is responsible for pulling the container images from the registry, starting and stopping the containers, and managing the containers resources. The coop proxy is a networking proxy that runs on each worker note. It is responsible for routing traffic to the correct parts. It also provides low balancing for the parts and ensures that the traffic is distributed evenly across the parts. So why should we use Kubernetes as with many things in software engineering? This is all about trade-offs. Let's look at the upsize first. Kubernetes is scalable and highly available. It provides features like self-healing, automatic, rollbacks, and horizontal scaling. It makes it easy to scale our application up and down as needed, allowing us to respond to changes in demand quickly. Kubernetes is portable. It helps us deploy and manage applications in a consistent and reliable way regardless of the underlying infrastructure. It runs on premise, in a public cloud, or in a hybrid environment. It provides a uniform way to package, deploy, and manage applications. Now, how about the downsides? The number one drawback is complexity. Kubernetes is complex to set up and operate. The upfront class is high, especially for organizations new to container orchestration. It requires a high level of expertise and resources to set up and manage a production Kubernetes environment. The second drawback is cost. Kubernetes requires a certain minimum level of resources to run in order to support all the features we mentioned about. It's likely an overkill for many smaller organizations. One popular option that strikes a reasonable balance is to offload the management of the control plane to a managed Kubernetes service. Management Kubernetes services are provided by cloud providers. Some popular ones are Amazon, EKS, GKE, and Google Cloud, and AKS on Azure. These services allow organizations to run Kubernetes applications without having to worry about the underlying infrastructure. They take care of tasks that we acquired deep expertise by setting up and configuring the control plane, scaling the cluster, and providing ongoing maintenance and support. This is a reasonable option for a mid-sized organization to test our Kubernetes. For a smaller organization, Yankney is our recommendation. If you would like to learn more about system design, check out books and weekly newsletter. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

为什么选择 Kubernetes？为什么它被称为 KAS？是什么让它如此受欢迎？让我们一探究竟。Kubernetes 是一个开源的容器编排平台。它彻底改变了容器化应用的部署、扩展和管理。Kubernetes 的历史可以追溯到谷歌内部的容器编排系统 Borg，该系统负责管理谷歌内部数千个应用的部署。2014 年，谷歌将这一成果的一个版本开源，也就是 Kubernetes。那么，为什么它被称为 KAS 呢？这是一种略显“极客”的缩写方式，用于缩短长词。KAS 中的数字 8 指的是 Kubernetes 一词中首字母 K 和末字母 S 之间的 8 个字母。其他类似的例子还有：i18n 代表国际化（internationalization），l10n 代表本地化（localization）。一个 Kubernetes 集群是由多个称为“节点”（nodes）的机器组成的集合，用于运行容器化应用。Kubernetes 集群包含两个核心部分。第一部分是控制平面（control plane），负责管理集群的状态。在生产环境中，控制平面通常运行在多个节点上，这些节点分布在多个数据中心区域中。第二部分是工作节点（worker nodes）的集合。这些节点负责运行容器化应用的工作负载。容器化应用在 Pod 中运行。Pod 是 Kubernetes 中最小的可部署单元。一个 Pod 可托管一个或多个容器，并为这些容器提供共享存储和网络功能。Pod 由 Kubernetes 控制平面创建和管理，是 Kubernetes 应用的基本构建模块。现在，让我们更深入地了解一下控制平面。控制平面由多个核心组件组成，包括 API 服务器、etcd、调度器和控制器管理器。API 服务器是控制平面与集群其余部分之间的主要接口。它提供了一个 RESTful API，使客户端能够与控制平面交互并提交请求来管理集群。etcd 是一个分布式键值存储，用于存储集群的系统状态。API 服务器及其他控制平面组件会利用它来存储和检索有关集群的信息。调度器负责将 Pod 调度到集群中的工作节点上。它会根据 Pod 所需的资源以及工作节点上的可用资源信息来做出调度决策。控制器管理器负责运行用于管理集群状态的控制器。例如，复制控制器可确保运行指定数量的 Pod 副本，而部署控制器则负责管理部署的滚动更新和回滚。接下来，让我们深入了解一下工作节点。在 Kubernetes 中，运行于工作节点的核心组件包括 Kubernetes、容器运行时和 kube-proxy。Kubernetes 是每个工作节点上运行的组件，负责与控制平面通信。它从控制平面接收关于应在该节点上运行哪些 Pod 的指令，并确保维持 Pod 的预期状态。容器运行时负责在节点上运行容器，其任务是将从注册表拉取容器镜像、启动和停止容器，以及管理容器的资源。kube-proxy 是运行在每个工作节点上的网络代理，负责将流量路由到正确的 Pod。它还能为 Pod 提供负载均衡，确保流量均匀地分配到各个 Pod 中。那么，为什么我们应该使用 Kubernetes 呢？正如软件工程中的许多其他技术一样，这完全取决于权衡。我们先来看看它的优势。Kubernetes 具备可扩展性和高可用性。它提供了自愈、自动回滚和水平扩展等功能，使我们能够根据需求轻松地扩展应用的规模，从而快速响应需求变化。Kubernetes 还具有可移植性。无论底层基础设施如何，它都能以一致且可靠的方式帮助我们部署和管理应用。它既可以在本地运行，也可以在公有云或混合环境中运行，为应用的打包、部署和管理提供了一种统一的方式。

那么，它的劣势又是什么呢？首要缺点是复杂性。Kubernetes 的部署和运维都非常复杂。对于刚接触容器编排的组织来说，初始的学习成本尤其高昂。要搭建和管理一个生产级的 Kubernetes 环境，需要高水平的专业知识和充足的资源支持。第二个缺点是成本。为了支持我们前面提到的所有功能，Kubernetes 需要达到一定的最低资源水平才能运行。对于许多规模较小的组织而言，它很可能显得有些大材小用。一个广受欢迎的选择是在控制平面的管理方面寻求合理的平衡，即将其卸载到托管 Kubernetes 服务。托管 Kubernetes 服务由云提供商提供。一些常见的服务包括 Amazon EKS、GKE、Google Cloud 以及 Azure 上的 AKS。这些服务使组织能够运行 Kubernetes 应用程序，而无需担心底层基础设施。它们负责处理那些我们曾通过设置和配置控制平面、扩展集群以及提供持续维护和支持而积累了深厚专业知识的任务。对于中型组织而言，这是一个测试 Kubernetes 的合理选择。对于规模较小的组织，我们推荐 Yankney。如果您想进一步了解系统设计，可以查看相关书籍和每周通讯。如果您学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P31 CI_CD In 5 Minutes _ Is It Worth The Hassle

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=31)　时长 5分47秒

**原文**

What is CICD? How does it help us ship faster? Is it worth the hassle? Let's take a look. CICD or continuous integration and continuous delivery all make the software development process from the initial code commit all the way through to deployment. It eliminates much of the manual human intervention traditionally required to get new code to production. The CICD process takes care of building, testing, and deploying new code to production. The promise is that it enables software teams to deploy better quality software faster. Now, this all sounds very good but does it work in real life? What the answer is? It depends. Let's break up CICD into their own ports and discuss them separately. CICD's less controversial and more common. In a nutshell, it is the practice of using automation to enable teams to merge code changes into the share repository early and often. Each commit triggers an automated workflow on a CICD server that runs a series of tasks to make sure the commit is safe to merge into the main branch. It goes CICD process relies on a good set of tests. It is not trivial to maintain a set of tests with sufficient coverage and that are not flaky. High test coverage usually takes longer to run. This impacts developer productivity. It is a tough balancing act but it's worth the effort to get it right. What are some common tools used in CI? A good source code management system is the foundation. Get help is a very popular example. You should hold everything needed to build a software. This includes source code, test scripts, and script to build a software applications. There are many tools to manage the CI process itself. Get up actions and build height are some modern examples. Jenkins, Circle CI, and Travis CI are also common. These tools manage the bill and test tasks in CI. There are many test tools for writing and running tests. These tools are usually language and ecosystem specific. For example, for JavaScript, Jess is an example of the unit testing framework, while playwright and cypress are some common integration testing frameworks for web applications. The bill tools are even more diverse and ecosystem specific. Greater is a powerful bill tool for Java. The JavaScript bill ecosystem is fragmented, and how to keep track of. Webpack is the standard, but many new bill tools claim to be much faster, but they are not yet as extensible as webpack. Now let's examine the CD part of CI CD. CD is continuous deployment. Now it will be truthful. Real continuous deployment is hard. They do exist, but the practice is not as common as CI. Many teams only practice CD on the most basic types of systems. These systems are usually stailers, like the API or web server tiers. With good production monitoring, these systems could be deployed continuously with minimal risks. They are stailers and rollbacks are usually quick and harmless. It's also a common practice to grab new features in feature flags to separate a deployment of the code from the activation of the features. This allows the team to quickly shut up new features if they cause any issues with that requiring a full rollback. Canary deployment is also common practice for products with hundreds of millions of users. It applies to a new production code to a tiny subset of the power users and staff who both appreciate new features and have the risk appetite to help catch problems before the new code is deployed widely. This allows the team to test the new code in a real world environment while limiting the blast radius if something goes wrong. These techniques work well for simple stailers systems. On the other hand, very few teams have the resources or the convictions to implement real continuous hand-off deployments on complex stailers systems like database back and clusters or other types of stailers systems like web socket clusters. Instead, these systems are usually on a fixed deploy cadence. The deployment process is manual, risky and time consuming. They require the care of a dedicated platform team. It is rare to see these systems deploy fully continuously and automatically. Now, what are some tools that are used for CD? The tools we mentioned earlier like GitHub actions, built-kai and Jenkins are commonly used to handle CD tests. There are also the infrastructure specific tools that make CD easier to maintain. For example, on Kubernetes, Oracle CD is popular. In conclusion, CICD is a powerful software development practice that can help teams ship better quality software faster. However, it is not a one-size-fits-all solution, and its implementation may vary depending on the complexity of the system. If you like to learn more about system design, check out our books and weekly newsletter. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

什么是 CICD？它如何帮助我们更快地发布？是否值得投入？让我们一起来看看。CICD，即持续集成与持续交付，涵盖了从最初代码提交到最终部署的整个软件开发流程。它消除了传统上新代码上线前所需的大量人工干预。CICD 流程负责构建、测试并将新代码部署到生产环境。其承诺是帮助软件团队更快地交付更高质量的软件。听起来很棒，但现实中是否真的有效呢？答案取决于具体情况。让我们将 CICD 拆分为其组成部分，分别进行讨论。CICD 中争议较少且更为普遍的部分是持续集成（CI）。简而言之，CI 就是通过自动化手段，鼓励团队频繁地将代码变更合并到共享仓库中。每次提交都会在 CICD 服务器上触发一个自动化工作流，执行一系列任务以确保该提交可以安全地合并到主分支。CICD 流程依赖于完善的测试套件。而要维护一套覆盖充分且稳定的测试并不容易。高测试覆盖率通常需要更长的运行时间，这会影响开发者的生产力。这是一个艰难的平衡过程，但努力将其做好是值得的。CI 中常用哪些工具？一个好的源代码管理系统是基础。Git 是一个非常流行的例子。你应该保留构建软件所需的一切内容，包括源代码、测试脚本以及构建软件应用的脚本。有许多工具可用于管理 CI 流程本身。GitHub Actions 和 Buildkite 是一些现代示例。Jenkins、CircleCI 和 Travis CI 也很常见。这些工具负责管理 CI 中的构建和测试任务。有许多用于编写和运行测试的测试工具，这些工具通常与特定语言和生态系统相关。例如，对于 JavaScript 来说，Jest 是一个单元测试框架的例子，而 Playwright 和 Cypress 则是用于 Web 应用的常见集成测试框架。构建工具则更加多样化且与生态系统相关。Maven 是 Java 的一个强大构建工具。JavaScript 的构建生态系统较为分散，如何对其进行跟踪管理也是一个问题。Webpack 是行业标准，但许多新的构建工具声称速度更快，不过它们的可扩展性还无法与 Webpack 相媲美。现在我们来了解一下 CI/CD 中的 CD 部分。CD 即持续交付（Continuous Delivery）。现在说点实在的：真正的持续交付其实很难实现。虽然确实存在这样的实践，但其普及程度远不及 CI。许多团队只在最基础类型的系统中实践 CD。这些系统通常是较为稳定的层级，比如 API 或 Web 服务器层。借助良好的生产环境监控，这些系统可以实现持续交付，同时风险极小。由于它们较为稳定，回滚通常既快速又无害。此外，一种常见做法是使用特性开关（feature flags）将代码的部署与特性的激活分离开来。这样，如果新特性引发问题，团队可以迅速将其禁用，而无需进行全面回滚。对于拥有数亿用户的公司而言，金丝雀发布（Canary deployment）也是一种常见做法。该做法会将新的生产环境代码仅应用于少量高级用户和内部员工——他们既乐于体验新功能，又愿意承担风险，帮助团队在新代码大规模部署前发现潜在问题。这使得团队能够在真实环境中测试新代码，同时将问题可能造成的影响范围限制在最小。这些技术对于简单的单体系统效果很好。另一方面，很少有团队具备资源或决心在复杂的单体系统（如数据库后端和集群，或其他类型的单体系统如 Web Socket 集群）上实施真正持续的手动交接部署。相反，这些系统通常采用固定的部署节奏。其部署过程是手动的、高风险且耗时的，需要专门的平台团队来维护。这些系统能够实现完全持续且自动部署的情况极为罕见。那么，有哪些工具可用于持续交付（CD）呢？我们前面提到的 GitHub Actions、Built-Ka 和 Jenkins 等工具常被用于处理 CD 测试。此外，还有一些针对基础设施的专用工具，使 CD 的维护更加便捷。例如，在 Kubernetes 上，Argo CD 就广受欢迎。总而言之，持续集成与持续交付（CI/CD）是一种强大的软件开发实践，能够帮助团队更快地交付更高质量的软件。然而，它并非适用于所有情况的通用解决方案，其实现方式可能因系统的复杂程度而异。如果您想了解更多关于系统设计的内容，可以查看我们的书籍和每周通讯。如果您学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P32 Why Is System Design Interview Important

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=32)　时长 2分54秒

**原文**

In this short video, we discussed why system design interviews are so important. System design interviews are a critical part of the hiring process for software engineers, particularly at top tech companies. Software is expensive to build. These companies need experienced engineers with strong system design skills. This increases their odds of building things right the first time. And system design interviews are a predominant method to gauge those skills. System design interviews are a way that much more heavily for senior engineers. For most well-known companies, staff level positions require multiple rounds of system designs. This all makes sense. The more senior we are in software engineering, the more system design matters in a job. Typically, in another way, how we perform in the system design rounds, greatly impacts the leveling and compensation decisions. Now, what do these companies look for in the system design rounds? And what are some of the dos and dos in these interviews? The number one signal these company look for is how we approach problem solving and how we think about designing large scale systems. They want to see if we can think critically, make and justify trade-offs between different design options, while balancing different constraints. They want to see whether we could communicate our ideas clearly. They want to see if we could work well with their teams, but with that in mind, here are some quick tips on dos and dos. Do understand the problem statement and requirements before designing. Often the question is deliberately vague. Do ask clarifying questions. Don't jump into solution without first understanding the problem. Do consider multiple designs, and clearly articulate trade-offs between them. Do consider scalability, latency, and other non-functional requirements. The higher the level, the more important are the non-functional aspects of the design. Don't over engineer or add unnecessary complexity. Do communicate clearly and explain the reasoning behind our design decisions. System design interviews are no dos stressful. This I hope that these short tips would be a good reminder for us all on how to approach these critical interviews. In future videos, we'll design some popular distributed systems, like WhatsApp, Facebook, News Feed, etc. Stay tuned. You would like to learn more about system design, check out our books and weekly newsletter. Please subscribe if you learned something new. Thank you so much and we'll see you next time.

**译文**

在这段简短的视频中，我们探讨了为什么系统设计面试如此重要。系统设计面试是软件工程师招聘过程中的关键一环，尤其是在顶尖科技公司。软件开发成本高昂，这些公司需要拥有扎实系统设计经验的工程师。这能提高他们一次性就构建出正确系统的成功率。而系统设计面试正是评估这些能力的主要方法。对于高级工程师而言，系统设计面试的比重要大得多。对于大多数知名公司，Staff级别的职位通常需要多轮系统设计面试。这一切都合乎逻辑：在软件工程中，我们的职级越高，系统设计在岗位中的重要性就越突出。通常来说，我们在系统设计环节的表现，会极大地影响职级和薪酬的决策。那么，这些公司在系统设计面试中究竟看重什么？面试中有哪些“该做”和“不该做”的事项呢？这些公司最看重的一点是：我们如何解决问题，以及我们如何思考大规模系统的设计。他们想看看我们是否能够进行批判性思考，能够在不同设计方案之间做出并合理论证权衡，同时平衡各种约束条件。他们想看看我们是否能够清晰地表达自己的想法。他们想看看我们是否能够与他们团队合作良好，但考虑到这一点，这里有一些关于“该做什么”和“不该做什么”的快速提示。在设计之前，务必理解问题陈述和需求。问题往往被刻意设计得比较模糊。要主动提问以澄清细节。不要在不先理解问题的情况下就急于给出解决方案。要考虑多种设计方案，并清楚地阐述它们之间的权衡。要考虑可扩展性、延迟以及其他非功能性需求。级别越高，设计的非功能性方面就越重要。不要过度设计或添加不必要的复杂性。要清晰地沟通，并解释我们做出设计决策背后的 reasoning。系统设计面试并不可怕。希望这些简短的提示能提醒我们所有人如何面对这些关键的面试。在未来的视频中，我们将设计一些常见的分布式系统，比如 WhatsApp、Facebook、News Feed 等。敬请期待。如果你想了解更多关于系统设计的知识，可以看看我们的书籍和每周通讯。如果你学到了新知识，请订阅。非常感谢，我们下次再见。

## P33 8 Key Data Structures That Power Modern Databases

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=33)　时长 4分34秒

**原文**

In this video, we uncovered a secret behind the modern databases we know and love. From my sequel to Redis to Cassandra, we break down the eight essential data structures that make it all possible. Next I write in. First up is skip list. A skip list is a probabilistic data structure that is used to implement a sort of set. It is an alternative to a balanced tree. This allows for efficient search, insertion, and deletion operations. In an in-memory database like Redis, a skip list is used to implement the order, data structure, such as sort of set. It allows for fast lookups, range queries, and other operations. Next is hash index. A hash index, also known as a hash table, is used to efficiently map keys to values by using a hash function to generate a hash value for each key. The hash value is used to quickly locate the value in the table, allowing for fast lookups, insertions, and deletions. Hash index is ubiquitous. This obviously is used to implement hash data structures by hashes in Redis, but is also widely used internally in all kinds of databases. The next two data structures go hand in hand. The SS table and the LSM tree. SS table or sort of string table. It is used to store data on disk in a sort of order. It is a file-based data structure that is used to store large amounts of data in a highly compressed and efficient format. SS table is a core component of the LSM tree. The other core component is a map table. Mamm table is an in-memory data structure that stores recent rights. SS table and Mamm table work together to handle a high volume of right operations. We have an entire video dedicated to the LSM tree. Check out the description below for a link to the video. LSM tree is the backbone of popular and no-secret databases, such as Apache's Cassandra, Rockstab and LevelDB. Next up is the B tree family. We include the B plus tree in this. The B tree family of data structures are used to efficiently store and retrieve large amounts of data on disk. B tree is a balance tree where each node can have multiple children and keeps data sorted. B plus tree is a specific type of B tree where all data is stored in leaf notes and internal notes only whole keys. They are widely used in databases such as mySQL, Postgres, and Oracle to handle large amounts of on this data. Next is inverter index. An inverter index is usually efficiently searched and retrieve data from a large collection of text documents. You create a mapping of words to documents in which they appear. The index is inverted because it maps the words to the documents rather than the other way around. Inverter indexes are commonly used in documents search engines by elastic search. Next is suffix tree. It is used in databases for efficient text searching. You quickly find all occurrences of a search term within a large collection of documents. The last data structure will discuss is somewhat specialized. It is archery. An archery is a spatial index data structure that organizes data based on their geometric boundaries, such as rectangles or polygons. It is used to efficiently store and retrieve spatial data in a database. It allows for fast spatial queries and is widely used in spatial databases that post GIS, MongoDB, and Elastic Search. This concludes our discussion of the eight data structures that power a modern data basis. This is by no mean and exhaustive list. Please let us know if there are others you would like us to discuss in the future. If you like our videos, you may like our weekly system design newsletter as well. It covers topics and trends in large-scale system designs. Trust up by 200,000 readers. Subscribe to that, block.bybiko.com

**译文**

在本视频中，我们揭示了现代数据库背后隐藏的秘密。从 Redis 的后续版本到 Cassandra，我们拆解了实现这一切的八种关键数据结构。接下来我将开始讲解。首先是跳表（skip list）。跳表是一种概率性数据结构，用于实现某种集合类型。它是平衡树的一种替代方案，能够实现高效的搜索、插入和删除操作。在像 Redis 这样的内存数据库中，跳表被用于实现有序数据结构，例如有序集合。它支持快速查找、范围查询及其他操作。接下来是哈希索引（hash index）。哈希索引（也称为哈希表）通过使用哈希函数为每个键生成哈希值，从而高效地将键映射到值。哈希值用于在表中快速定位值，从而实现快速的查找、插入和删除操作。哈希索引无处不在。它显然被用于实现 Redis 中的哈希数据结构，同时也被广泛应用于各类数据库的内部实现中。接下来的两种数据结构总是相辅相成：SS 表和 LSM 树。SS 表，即排序字符串表（sort of string table）。它用于以某种顺序将数据存储到磁盘中。它是一种基于文件的数据结构，用于以高度压缩且高效的形式存储大量数据。SS 表是 LSM 树的核心组件之一。另一个核心组件是 Map 表。Map 表是一种内存数据结构，用于存储最近写入的数据。SS 表和 Map 表协同工作，以处理大量写入操作。我们有一个完整的视频专门讲解 LSM 树。请查看下方描述中的视频链接。LSM 树是许多流行且广为人知的数据库（如 Apache 的 Cassandra、Rocksteady 和 LevelDB）的支柱。接下来是 B 树家族，其中也包括 B+ 树。B 树家族的数据结构用于在磁盘中高效地存储和检索大量数据。B 树是一种平衡树，每个节点可以有多个子节点，并保持数据有序。B+ 树是 B 树的一种特殊类型，所有数据都存储在叶子节点中，而内部节点仅存储键。它们被广泛应用于 MySQL、Postgres 和 Oracle 等数据库中，以处理大量的磁盘数据。接下来是倒排索引。倒排索引通常可以高效地搜索并从大量文本文档中检索数据。你创建一个将词语映射到其出现文档的映射关系。该索引之所以称为“倒排”，是因为它将词语映射到文档，而不是相反。倒排索引通常被 Elasticsearch 等文档搜索引擎广泛使用。接下来是后缀树。它被用于数据库中以实现高效的文本搜索。你可以快速找到大型文档集合中某个搜索词的所有出现位置。最后要讨论的数据结构有些特殊，它就是 R 树。R 树是一种空间索引数据结构，它根据数据的几何边界（如矩形或多边形）来组织数据。它被用于在数据库中高效地存储和检索空间数据，支持快速的空间查询，并广泛应用于 PostGIS、MongoDB 和 Elasticsearch 等空间数据库中。至此，我们关于支撑现代数据库的八种数据结构的讨论就结束了。这绝非一份详尽无遗的列表。如果您希望我们在未来讨论其他数据结构，请随时告知。如果您喜欢我们的视频，可能也会喜欢我们的每周系统设计时事通讯。它涵盖了大规模系统设计的相关主题和趋势。已赢得 20 万读者的信赖。欢迎订阅：block.bybiko.com

## P34 System Design Interview_ A Step-By-Step Guide

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=34)　时长 9分54秒

**原文**

In this video, we talk about the system design interview framework that we use in a books. We explain why you may want to consider using something similar when you interview. Now, let's first talk about why we may want to follow a framework. Interviews are stressful. There's very little time to show up what we are capable of. Without any structure, it is easy to waste precious time on things that don't matter. An unstructured interview makes it difficult for our interviewer to follow. Our framework provides a time test structure. It is simple and powerful. It is as to ask the right question and focus on the right things. Here are the four steps of our framework. Step one, understand the problem and establish design school. Step two, propose high level design and get by in. Step three, design deep dive, and step four is wrap up. This framework works well for a typical 45 to 16 minutes long system design interview session. Usually the first five minutes is an introduction and the last five is Q&A. The meat of the interview is only about 35 to 45 minutes. We suggest allocating about five minutes to step one, 20 minutes to step two, 15 minutes to step three, and five minutes to step four. Now keep in mind this is just a rough guideline feel free to adjust as needed. Let's take a closer look at each of the four steps. Step one, understand the problem and establish design school. System design questions are usually open-ended. Sometimes they are presented in a way that is deliberately vague. This tests our ability to organize our thoughts and focus on what is important. It is our job to ask as many questions as necessary to understand the problem fully. It is a rough flag to jump right into a solution with our first understanding what we are building. What questions do we ask? Our goal is to clarify the requirements. We want to understand why we are building the system, who the users are and what features we need to build. For example, we are asked to design a chat app. It is important to recognize that there are many different types of chat app in the marketplace. They are one-on-one and small group chat apps like WhatsApp, office chat apps like Slack or group chat apps like Discord. They have different design challenges and constraints. To goal is to understand the features we are building in priority order. We should focus on the top few features to build, make sure the interviewer agrees to the feature list. It is also important to ask a series of questions to clarify the non-functional requirements. For system design interviews, we suggest focusing on scale and performance. These non-functional requirements are what make our design unique and challenging. For example, designing a Twitter clone to support a few hundred users is easy. While designing it for hundreds of millions of users, with some popular accounts having millions of followers is interesting and challenging. Now the more senior the role is, the more important it is for us to demonstrate our ability to handle the non-functional requirements. Now to help us get a sense of the scale of the system and the potential challenges and bottlenecks, we might do some rough back of the envelope calculations here. Do you mind that from the potential challenging? So the map we do here is a rough estimate. To goal is to get a general sense of the scale. We want to get the order of the magnitude right. Now this step should take about five minutes. At the end, we should have a short list of features to design for, along with a few important non-functional requirements to satisfy. For step two of our framework, we aim to develop a high-level design and reach an agreement with the interviewer on the design. For most designs, we would suggest a top-down approach and start with the APIs. The APIs establish a contract between the end users and the back-end systems. You should be clear after gathering the requirements what APIs we would need. Unless it is specified otherwise, we should follow the restful convention. Define each APIs input parameters and output responses carefully. Take the time to carefully review the APIs, verify that they satisfy the functional requirements. Equally important to remember, do not introduce APIs that have nothing to do with the functional requirements. An additional consideration on API, some designs may call for two-way communication between client and server. Now in this scenario, WebSocket is a common solution here. Be mindful that a socket service that WebSocket is usually faithful. It is quite challenging to operate a scale. If scale is high, be prepared to discuss how we would manage a WebSocket deployment in the deep dive section. Once we have established the APIs between a client and a server, the next step is to lay out the high-level design diagram. The diagram is a blueprint of the overall design that we can refer back to. We should use it to verify that the design satisfies all the feature requirements and to end. For many designs, you start with a low-bouncer or an API gateway. Behind that are the services that will satisfy the feature requirements we established earlier with the inner viewer. Many services require some form of persistence. This is where we would round out a design by introducing the data storage layer. It is usually not necessary to specify the exact database technology to use at this stage. This should be deferred to the deep-ly section if necessary, and only after we have designed the data schema. For example, for a map service by Google Maps, the client often needs to send frequent GPS updates to the server. We include a design and location data store that a location update service right to. Repeat the steps above and complete the high-level design diagram for all the major features. Here's a pro tip. While developing the high-level design, maintain a list of discussion points for later. Resist the temptations to dig into too much detail too early. Do not dig ourselves into a hole before we have a full picture of the design. The last step of the high-level design is to hash out the data model and schema. Here we should discuss the data access patterns and the read-write ratio. As scale, data modeling could significantly impact the performance of the design. It is simple. Also discuss the databases to choose and maybe discuss the indexing options. Here we should make some judgment calls. The data modeling is to key part of the design to satisfy the non-functional requirements. We might want to defer the discussion to the deep-dive section. When we are done with the high-level design, take a step back and review the design. Make sure each feature is complete and to end. Here we reach the third step of the framework design deep-dive. To go of this section, is to demonstrate that we could identify areas that could potentially be problematic and come over with solutions and discuss trade-offs. We should work with the interviewer closely to decide what to discuss in depth. Deep-dive is where non-functional requirements make the problem interesting. The higher the level, the more important this section is. The section is really open-ended. There is no one-size-fits-all approach. Here is where the ability to read the room is useful. Sometimes the interviewer's body language would give a way clues that they are dissatisfied with certain aspect of the design. It is important to pick up these clues and make sure the issues are addressed. One way we could approach it is by asking questions. We could list out the reasons for choosing a particular solution and ask if they have any questions or concerns. Once we pick out a problem or two to dive deeper into, we should come over multiple solutions and discuss trade-offs for each option. We could use the following many guidelines when framing the discussions. First, we should clearly articulate a problem. For example, for designing Google Maps, the right QPS at 1 million per second to the location databases too high for single database to handle. Next, we come over with at least two solutions. Continuing with the example above, we could propose to reduce the location update frequency per user or choose a no-cycle database that could handle the right rate. Third, we discussed the trade-offs of the solutions. Remember to use numbers to back up our design. Finally, we pick a solution and discuss it with the interviewer. Now repeat this for other problems. In a typical interview, we should only have time to dive deeper into the top two or three issues. Lastly, we have reached the last step, wrap up. We suggest spending a few minutes summarizing the design. Here we should focus on the parts that are unique to the particular situation. Keep this short and sweet. Leave you never room at the end of the interview to ask the interviewer questions about the company. That's it on the System Design Interview Framework. We hope you find it useful. If you like our videos, you may like our weekly system design newsletter as well. It covers topics and trends in large-scale system designs, trusted by 200,000 readers. Subscribe to that block.bybikeol.com.

**译文**

在本视频中，我们将讨论我们在书中使用的系统设计面试框架。我们会解释为什么你在面试时可以考虑使用类似的框架。现在，我们先来谈谈为什么要遵循一个框架。面试压力很大，而展示自己能力的时间却非常有限。如果没有结构，我们很容易把宝贵的时间浪费在不重要的事情上。而没有结构的面试也会让面试官难以跟上思路。我们的框架提供了一个经过时间检验的结构。它简单而强大，旨在引导我们提出正确的问题并专注于关键点。以下是我们框架的四个步骤：第一步，理解问题并确立设计目标；第二步，提出高层设计并获得认可；第三步，深入设计细节；第四步，总结收尾。这个框架适用于典型的45分钟系统设计面试。通常，前5分钟是介绍，后5分钟是问答环节。面试的核心部分只有大约35到45分钟。我们建议将大约五分钟分配给第一步，20分钟分配给第二步，15分钟分配给第三步，以及五分钟分配给第四步。请注意，这只是一个粗略的指导方针，您可以根据需要自由调整。让我们更详细地看看这四个步骤。第一步：理解问题并确定设计方向。系统设计问题通常是开放式的，有时会以故意模糊的方式呈现。这考验了我们整理思路并专注于重点的能力。我们的职责是提出尽可能多的问题，以全面理解问题。如果仅凭初步理解就草率地开始寻找解决方案，那将是一个明显的失误。我们应该问哪些问题呢？我们的目标是明确需求。我们需要了解为什么要构建这个系统、用户是谁，以及需要实现哪些功能。例如，如果我们被要求设计一个聊天应用，就必须认识到市面上存在多种不同类型的聊天应用：有像WhatsApp这样的单聊和小群组聊天应用，有像Slack这样的办公聊天应用，也有像Discord这样的群组聊天应用。它们有不同的设计挑战和限制。我们的目标是了解正在构建的功能，并按优先级排序。我们应该专注于构建最重要的几个功能，并确保面试官同意该功能列表。同时，提出一系列问题以明确非功能性需求也很重要。对于系统设计面试，我们建议重点关注规模和性能。这些非功能性需求正是使我们的设计独特且富有挑战性的关键。例如，设计一个可支持数百用户的 Twitter 克隆版很容易；而设计一个可支持数亿用户、其中一些热门账号拥有数百万粉丝的系统，则更具趣味性和挑战性。职位越高，展示我们处理非功能性需求的能力就越重要。为了帮助我们了解系统的规模以及潜在的挑战和瓶颈，我们可以在这里进行一些粗略的估算。您介意我们从潜在挑战的角度出发吗？因此，我们在这里所做的估算只是粗略的。目标是获得对规模的总体感知，确保数量级是正确的。现在这一步大约需要五分钟。最后，我们应该会得到一个简短的功能设计列表，以及需要满足的一些重要非功能性需求。在我们框架的第二步中，目标是制定一个高层设计，并与面试官就该设计达成一致。对于大多数设计，我们建议采用自上而下的方法，从 API 开始。API 建立了最终用户与后端系统之间的契约。在收集完需求后，你应该清楚我们需要哪些 API。除非另有说明，否则应遵循 RESTful 规范。仔细定义每个 API 的输入参数和输出响应。花时间仔细审查这些 API，验证它们是否满足功能性需求。同样重要的是，不要引入与功能性需求无关的 API。关于 API 的另一个考虑是，某些设计可能需要客户端与服务器之间的双向通信。在这种情况下，WebSocket 是一种常用的解决方案。需要注意的是，WebSocket 所使用的套接字服务通常较为复杂，要实现规模化运营颇具挑战性。如果规模较大，请准备好在深入探讨部分讨论我们应如何管理 WebSocket 部署。一旦我们确定了客户端和服务器之间的 API，下一步就是绘制高层设计图。该图是整体设计的蓝图，我们可以随时参考。我们应该用它来验证设计是否满足所有功能需求，并以此结束。对于许多设计而言，你通常会从一个低门槛组件或 API 网关开始。在其背后是能够满足我们先前与内部查看器一起确立的功能需求的服务。许多服务需要某种形式的持久化存储。这时，我们可以通过引入数据存储层来完成设计。在此阶段，通常不需要指定要使用的确切数据库技术。如有必要，这应推迟到深入探讨部分，并且只有在设计好数据模式之后才能进行。例如，对于 Google Maps 提供的地图服务，客户端通常需要向服务器发送频繁的 GPS 更新。我们会加入一个设计和位置数据存储，供位置更新服务写入。重复上述步骤，为所有主要功能完善高层设计图。这里有一个专业建议：在制定高层设计时，请维护一份待讨论事项清单。避免过早陷入过多细节的诱惑，切勿在未掌握完整设计概貌前将自己引入困境。高层设计的最后一步是明确数据模型和模式。在此阶段，我们应讨论数据访问模式以及读写比例。随着规模扩大，数据建模可能对设计性能产生显著影响。这部分内容相对简单。此外，还需讨论所选数据库，并可能涉及索引选项。在此环节，我们需要做出一些判断决策。数据建模是满足非功能性需求的关键设计部分。我们或许可以将相关讨论推迟到深入探讨阶段。完成高层设计后，请退后一步进行全面审查，确保每个功能都完整无缺。至此，我们便进入了框架设计深入探讨的第三步。本节的目标是展示我们能够识别可能存在问题的领域，并提出解决方案，同时讨论各种权衡。我们应该与面试官密切合作，决定深入探讨哪些内容。深入探讨环节正是非功能性需求使问题变得有趣的地方。层级越高，本节就越重要。本节内容实际上非常开放，没有放之四海而皆准的方法。这时，读懂现场氛围的能力就显得很有用。有时，面试官的肢体语言会透露出他们对设计某些方面不满意的信息。捕捉这些信号并确保相关问题得到解决非常重要。一种可行的方法是提出问题。我们可以列出选择某一特定解决方案的理由，然后询问他们是否有任何问题或担忧。一旦确定了一两个需要深入探讨的问题，我们就应该提出多种解决方案，并讨论每种方案的权衡。在组织讨论时，我们可以遵循以下指导原则：首先，应清晰地阐明问题。例如，在设计 Google Maps 时，每秒 100 万次的位置数据库查询请求（QPS）对于单个数据库来说过高。接下来，我们提出至少两种解决方案。以上述例子继续说明，我们可以建议降低每个用户的位置更新频率，或选择能够处理该请求速率的无环数据库。第三，我们讨论了这些解决方案的权衡取舍。记得用数据来支持我们的设计。最后，我们选定一个解决方案并与面试官讨论。现在针对其他问题重复这个过程。在一次典型的面试中，我们通常只能深入探讨两到三个最重要的问题。最后，我们进入最后一步：总结。我们建议花几分钟时间对设计方案进行总结。此时应重点说明那些针对特定情境的独特部分。保持简洁明了。在面试结束前，务必留出时间向面试官询问有关公司的问题。以上就是系统设计面试框架的全部内容。我们希望您觉得它有用。如果您喜欢我们的视频，可能也会喜欢我们的每周系统设计简报。它涵盖了大型系统设计的主题和趋势，深受20万读者信赖。订阅 ## P35 Top 5 Redis Use Cases

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=35)　时长 6分28秒

**原文**

In this video, we discovered the versatility of Redis. We'll talk about the top use cases that have been better tested in production, a various companies, and a various scales. Getting insights into how Redis solves interesting skills and ability challenges for us. And learn why it is a great tool to know well in our system design tool set. Let's dive right into it. First of all, what is Redis and why do people use it? Redis is an in-memory data structure store, it's most commonly used as a cache. It supports many data structures, such as strings, hashes, lists, sets, and sort of sets. Redis is known for its speed, we may have video to explain why Redis is so fast. Look for the link in the description you would like to watch that video. Now let's dive into the top use cases of Redis. The number one use case, Redis, is caching objects to speed up web applications. In this use case, Redis source frequently requests the data in memory. It allows the web servers to return frequently accessed data quickly. This reduces the low on the database and improves the response time for the application. As scale, the cache is distributed among a cluster of Redis servers. Harding is a common technique to distribute and caching low evenly across the cluster. Other topics to consider when deploying Redis as a distributed cache include setting a correct TTL and handling thundering herd on co-start. Another common use case is to use Redis as a session store to share session data among stateless servers. When a user locks into a web application, the session data is stored in Redis, along with a unique session ID that is returned to the client as a cookie. When a user makes a request to the application, the session ID is included in the request, and the stateless web server retrieves the session data from Redis using the ID. Now it is important to note that Redis is an in-memory database. The session data stored in Redis will be lost if the Redis servers restarts. Even though Redis provides persistent options by a snapshot and AOF or a Pan-only file, they allow session data to be saved to disk and reload in the memory in the event of a restart. These options often takes too long to low to be practical. In production, the application is usually used instead. In this case, data is replicated to a backup instance. In the event of a crash of the Ming instance, the backup instance is quickly promoted to take over the traffic. The next use case is Distributed Lock. Distributed Locks are used when multiple notes in an application need to coordinate access to some share resource. Redis is used as a Distributed Lock, with its atomic commands lie set and next, or set if not exists. It allows a caller to set a key, only if it does not already exist. This is how it works at a high level. Client 1 tries to acquire the lock by setting a key with a unique value and a time out using the set and X command. If the key was not already set, the set and X command returns 1, indicating that the lock has been acquired by Client 1. Client 1 finishes its work and releases the lock by deleting the key. Now if the key was already set, the set and X command returns 0. indicating that the lock has already been held by another Client. In this case, Client 1 waits and retries the set and X operation, until the lock is released by the other Client. Note that this simple implementation might be good enough for many use cases, but it's not completely fault-tolerant. For production use, there are many redis-client libraries that provide high quality distributed lock implementation built right up the box. Next up is Ray Limiter. Redis can be used as a Ray Limiter by using its increment command on some counters and setting expiration times on those counters. A very basic Ray Limiting algorithm works this way. For each incoming request, the request IP or use ID is used as a key. The number request for the key is increment to using the increment command in redis. The current count is compared to the allow rate limit. If the count is within the Ray Limit, the request is processed. If the count is over the limit, the request is rejected. The key is set to expire after a specific time window. For example, a minute, to reset the counts for the next time window. Most sophisticated Ray Limiter's, like the leaky bucket algorithm, can also be implemented using redis. The last new case we would like to talk about is gaming leaderboard. For most games, they are not super high-scale. Redis is a delightful way to implement various types of gaming leaderboards. Soda sets are the fundamental data structure that enables this. A Soda set is a collection of unique elements, each with a score associated with it. The elements are sorted by score. This allows for quick retrieval of the elements by score in logarithmic time. That's it for our top Redis use cases. Whereas it's very versatile. There are many different ways to use it. Some features are more better tested than others. Our creativity is a limit. Use it with care and have fun with it. You will like our videos. You may like our weekly system design newsletter as well. It covers topics and trends in large-scale system design. Trust it by 200,000 readers. Subscribe that blog.bybiko.com.

**译文**

在本视频中，我们探讨了 Redis 的多种用途。我们将讨论在生产环境中经过充分验证的主要使用场景，这些场景来自不同公司和不同规模的项目。通过了解 Redis 如何解决一些有趣的技能和能力挑战，我们将认识到它为何值得成为我们系统设计工具包中的重要工具。现在，让我们直接开始吧。首先，什么是 Redis？为什么人们要使用它？Redis 是一个内存数据结构存储系统，最常用于缓存。它支持多种数据结构，例如字符串（strings）、哈希（hashes）、列表（lists）、集合（sets）以及有序集合（sorted sets）。Redis 以其速度著称，我们可能有一个视频来解释 Redis 为何如此快速。请在描述中查找链接，观看该视频。现在，让我们深入探讨 Redis 的主要使用场景。排名第一的使用场景是：使用 Redis 缓存对象以加速 Web 应用。在这种使用场景中，Redis 将频繁请求的数据存储在内存中，使 Web 服务器能够快速返回这些数据。这减少了数据库的负载，并提升了应用的响应时间。随着规模扩大，缓存会分布在 Redis 服务器集群中。Harding 是一种常用的技术，用于在集群中均匀地分配和缓存数据。在将 Redis 用作分布式缓存时，需要考虑的其他主题包括设置正确的 TTL 以及处理多个节点同时启动时出现的“雷暴群”（thundering herd）问题。另一个常见的用例是将 Redis 用作会话存储，以在无状态服务器之间共享会话数据。当用户登录 Web 应用程序时，会话数据会存储在 Redis 中，同时返回一个唯一的会话 ID 作为 Cookie 给客户端。当用户向应用程序发出请求时，会话 ID 会包含在请求中，无状态 Web 服务器使用该 ID 从 Redis 中检索会话数据。需要注意的是，Redis 是一个内存数据库。如果 Redis 服务器重启，存储在 Redis 中的会话数据将会丢失。尽管 Redis 提供了通过快照、AOF 或仅追加文件（Pan-only file）实现的持久化选项，这些选项可以将会话数据保存到磁盘，并在重启时重新加载到内存中，但这些选项通常耗时过长，不够实用。在生产环境中，通常会采用其他方法。在这种情况下，数据会被复制到备份实例中。如果 Ming 实例发生故障，备份实例会迅速被提升以接管流量。下一个用例是分布式锁。当应用程序中的多个节点需要协调对某个共享资源的访问时，就会使用分布式锁。Redis 被用作分布式锁，其原子命令（如 `set` 和 `setnx`，或 `set if not exists`）可让调用者仅在键不存在时才设置一个键。其工作原理大致如下：客户端 1 尝试通过 `set` 和 `X` 命令设置一个带有唯一值和超时的键来获取锁。如果该键尚未被设置，`set` 和 `X` 命令将返回 1，表示客户端 1 已成功获取锁。客户端 1 完成工作后，通过删除该键来释放锁。如果该键已被设置，`set` 和 `X` 命令将返回 0，表示锁已被其他客户端持有。在这种情况下，客户端 1 会等待并重新尝试 `set` 和 `X` 操作，直到其他客户端释放锁为止。请注意，这种简单的实现可能对许多用例来说已经足够，但它并非完全具备容错能力。在生产环境中，有许多 redis-client 库提供了开箱即用的高质量分布式锁实现。接下来是 Ray Limiter。Redis 可以通过对其计数器使用 increment 命令并为这些计数器设置过期时间，被用作 Ray Limiter。一个非常基础的 Ray Limiting 算法工作原理如下：对于每个传入的请求，使用请求的 IP 或用户 ID 作为键。使用 Redis 中的 increment 命令对该键的请求计数进行递增。将当前计数与允许速率限制进行比较。如果计数在 Ray Limit 范围内，则处理该请求；如果计数超过限制，则拒绝该请求。然后将该键设置为在特定时间窗口（例如一分钟）后过期，以便为下一个时间窗口重置计数。大多数更复杂的 Ray Limiter（如漏桶算法）也可以通过 Redis 实现。我们想讨论的最后一个新用例是游戏排行榜。对于大多数游戏而言，其规模并非超大规模。Redis 是实现各种类型游戏排行榜的绝佳方式。Sorted Set 是实现这一功能的基础数据结构。Soda 集是一种包含唯一元素的集合，每个元素都关联一个分数。这些元素按分数排序。这使得可以通过分数以对数时间快速检索元素。以上就是我们介绍的主要 Redis 使用场景。尽管它非常灵活，有多种不同的使用方式。某些功能比其他功能经过更充分的测试。我们的创造力是唯一的限制。请谨慎使用，尽情享受吧。您会喜欢我们的视频，可能也会喜欢我们的每周系统设计通讯。它涵盖了大规模系统设计的相关主题和趋势。已有 20 万读者信赖该通讯。请订阅博客：blog.bybiko.com。
