If you ever wonder why Debugging is not taught in school, most developers actually learn it on the job and only a lucky few have a mentor to guide them through the process. In this video, we're going to present a systematic approach to Debugging. They can use as a checklist on your Debugging journey. Debugging demands discipline and is easy to get off course and ways valuable time. We'll show you how to mix on trade-offs and use good judgment. Whether you're a beginner or an experienced developer, this video is for you. Follow along as we share our tips and tricks for effective Debugging. And remember, free free to use these in any order. There's no right or wrong way to debug. Let's start by discussing the importance of having the right mindset when it comes to debugging challenging problems. You can mean a difference between giving up to early or ultimately solving the issue. When facing top debugging challenges, it's important to keep in mind the following things. First, remember the computers the logical, and there's always a logical explanation for the issue at hand, even if it may seem impossible to find at the moment. Second, being stuck is only temporary, and with persistence and effort, the issue we eventually be resolved. Third, it's important to know your limits and recognize when it's time to seek help from others who may have more expertise. Finally, it's not always necessary to spend a significant amount of effort in re-solving every bug. We should prioritize bugs based on their potential impact and severity. We should accept the fact that some bugs may not be worth the effort. Now, let's start by covering the basics. When we receive a bug report from a customer, it's important to gather as much information about it as possible. Request a screenshot or a screen recording from the customer if possible. Additionally, collect detailed steps to reproduce the issue, and gather all relevant logs, including any error messages associated with the bug. The goal is to obtain as much information as possible to help us reproduce the problem. We can isolate the environment and context, whether issue occurs, we can try to recreate it on a staging server and see if we could reproduce the problem. Having a reproducible environment is half the battle. Now that we have a reproducible environment, how should we investigate a bug, here are few strategies to consider. First, use print statements liberally. They can help construct a timeline of what happened, and determine if the event we expected to happen, in the co, actually align with the print statements. For some language ecosystems, setting up a debugger may be a viable option. For example, systems that are early in OTP have excellent introspection capability, making the debugger an excellent first tool to reach for. Ultimately, we can reproduce the bug. You should not be too difficult to get to the bottom of it. Oh, take time and patience, following the strategies that we mentioned about, should solve the issue eventually. Now what if we can reproduce the issue? Or there's much tougher, you may require some luck and a lot of patience. Let's try to understand why we sometimes have so much trouble reproducing the issue. Here are a few common scenarios. Some bugs only appear under production loads, and some bugs appear under production load, only in certain race conditions. There may be specific contacts or environment on the customer's device that triggers the bug. What can we do in these situations? Here are a few ideas. If there's a specific error, we trace the call from the line where the error occur all the way up the cost act, come through all the logs for clues and build a timeline by following one fail we request through the entire request lifecycle. If we're lucky, we may be able to come up with a theory as to what is happening. Next, we can add logging to the code to prove the theory and deploy it to production or ship it to the customer. Repeat this process until we get a more clues, and eventually solve the issue. Keep in mind that this cycle can be lengthy and frustrating, but persistence is key. Finally, let's review some general strategies to consider when we are completely stuck. Take a break. Sometimes stepping away from the problem and coming back with a fresh mind can provide new insights. Sleep on it, get some exercise or engage in a different activity to clear your mind. Get a rubber duck. Sometimes talking about the problem out loud can need to a light bulb moment. For others, writing out the problem or emailing and imaginary mentor about it can help generate new ideas and perspectives. Get help. Don't hesitate to collaborate with someone else on the problem. If fresh set of eyes and a different perspective can often help to reveal new solutions. The bugging can be tough, but with the right mindset and strategies, you can overcome any challenge. Good luck. You like our videos. You may like our weekly system design newsletter as well. It covers topics and trends in large-scale system design, trusted by 250,000 readers. Subscribe that. **译文**

如果你曾好奇为什么学校里不教调试（Debugging），那么大多数开发者其实是在工作中学习这项技能的，只有少数幸运儿有导师指导他们完成整个过程。在本视频中，我们将介绍一种系统化的调试方法，你可以将其作为调试之旅上的检查清单。调试需要纪律性，很容易偏离正轨并浪费宝贵时间。我们将向你展示如何在各种权衡之间取得平衡，并运用良好的判断力。无论你是初学者还是经验丰富的开发者，这个视频都适合你。请跟随我们一起分享有效调试的技巧和窍门。请记住，你可以自由地按任意顺序使用这些方法。调试没有对错之分。让我们首先讨论一下，在面对具有挑战性的问题时，拥有正确心态的重要性。这种心态可能决定了你是过早放弃，还是最终解决问题。当面对最棘手的调试挑战时，请记住以下几点：首先，要记住计算机是逻辑的，任何问题背后总有一个合乎逻辑的解释，即使目前看起来似乎不可能找到它。其次，卡住只是暂时的，只要坚持不懈并付出努力，问题最终终将得到解决。第三，了解自己的极限并在需要时向更有专长的他人寻求帮助，这一点非常重要。最后，并非总是需要投入大量精力去修复每一个错误。我们应该根据错误的潜在影响和严重程度进行优先级排序。我们应该接受这样一个事实：有些错误可能并不值得投入那么多精力。现在，让我们从基础开始讲起。当我们收到客户提交的错误报告时，尽可能多地收集相关信息非常重要。如果可能的话，请客户提供屏幕截图或屏幕录像。此外，收集详细的复现步骤，并整理所有相关的日志，包括与该错误相关的任何错误消息。我们的目标是尽可能多地获取信息，以帮助我们复现问题。我们可以隔离环境和上下文，无论问题出现在何处，我们都可以尝试在预发布服务器上重现该问题。拥有一个可复现的环境，就成功了一半。既然我们已经拥有了一个可复现的环境，接下来该如何排查一个 bug 呢？这里有几个策略可供参考。首先，可以频繁地使用 print 语句。它们有助于构建事件发生的时间线，并判断我们预期发生的事件是否确实与 print 语句的输出一致。对于某些语言生态系统来说，设置调试器可能是一个可行的选择。例如，处于 OTP 早期阶段的系统通常具备出色的自省能力，因此调试器是首选的绝佳工具。归根结底，只要能够复现 bug，就不应该很难查明其根源。哦，请保持耐心和从容，按照我们前面提到的策略去做，问题最终应该能够得到解决。那么，如果我们无法复现问题该怎么办呢？或者问题更加棘手，可能需要一些运气和极大的耐心。让我们来试着理解一下，为什么有时我们会如此难以复现问题。以下是几种常见的情况：有些 bug 只在生产环境的负载下才会出现；有些则是在生产负载下，仅在特定的竞争条件下才会显现。此外，客户设备上的某些特定配置或环境也可能触发该 bug。在这些情况下，我们可以做些什么呢？以下是一些思路。如果出现特定错误，我们可以从错误发生的行开始追溯调用栈，一路向上检查所有日志寻找线索，并通过跟踪单个请求的整个生命周期来构建时间线。如果运气好的话，我们或许能够推测出问题的成因。接下来，我们可以在代码中添加日志以验证这一推测，然后将其部署到生产环境或交付给客户。重复这一过程，直到获得更多线索并最终解决问题。请记住，这个循环可能既漫长又令人沮丧，但坚持是关键。最后，让我们回顾一些在完全卡住时可以尝试的通用策略。休息一下。有时，暂时离开问题，换一种思路再回来，可能会带来新的启发。可以睡一觉、做些运动或从事其他活动来清空思绪。准备一个“橡胶鸭”。有时，把问题大声说出来，可能会突然灵光一闪。对其他人而言，写下问题或给一位想象中的导师发送邮件，也有助于激发新的想法和视角。寻求帮助。不要犹豫，与别人一起合作解决问题。新的视角和不同的观点往往能帮助揭示新的解决方案。调试可能很棘手，但只要有正确的心态和策略，你就能克服任何挑战。祝你好运。如果你喜欢我们的视频，可能也会喜欢我们的每周系统设计通讯。它涵盖了大规模系统设计的话题和趋势，深受 25 万读者的信赖。立即订阅吧。Block.buy-buy-go.com。

## P37 But What Is Cloud Native Really All About

[原视频](https://www.bilibili.com/video/BV1yt4y137cN?p=37)　时长 7分32秒

**原文**

What is cloud native? How is it different than cloud computing? Should we go cloud native? Let's take a closer look. The term cloud native seemed to first appear around 10 years ago. When Netflix discussed their web scale application architecture at a 2013 AWS reinvent talk. At that time, the meaning of the term was slightly different than it is today. However, one thing remains the same. There were no clear definitions for it, and there are still no clear definitions now. It means different things to different people. In this video, we provide our interpretation of the term cloud native, and discuss why and when it is important. To start, what are the promises of cloud native? Cloud native is a blueprint for building web scale applications on the cloud that are more available and scalable. It promises increase agility to ship new features quickly without compromising availability, making it quicker to respond to changing customer demands. Before we dive into how cloud native fulfills these promises, let's first understand what is close cousin cloud computing is. In this most basic form, cloud computing is running applications on computing resources managed by cloud providers without having to purchase and manage hardware ourselves. Migrating and existing monolithic application from on-premise to the cloud is a great start. It frees the team for managing hardware infrastructure. This fast-upposition new computing resources and scaling up this effortless. However, simply running an application on the cloud does not make it cloud native. For an application to be considered cloud native, they are at least four pillars to consider. The first pillar is the application architecture. Cloud native applications are made of a multiple small independent services called microservices. Traditionally, developers build monolithic applications with a single binary containing all the required functionalities. It was difficult to develop, test, and deploy a monolithic application quickly. It was also challenging to scale in a monolithic application. By using the cloud native approach, developers sprig the functionalities of a large application into smaller microservices. These services are designed to be small, allowing teams to take ownership of their own services, and deploy and scale them independently on their own timeline. These microservices are loosely coupled. They communicate with each other via well-defined APIs. For example, an e-commerce application might be composed of a shopping cart service which talks to a payment service and an inventory service. The second pillar is containers and container orchestration. Cloud native applications are packaged and containers. Containers are lightweight components that contain everything needed to run a microservice in any environment. Container orchestration is an essential component for large cloud native applications. As a number of microservices grows, container orchestration manages to large number of containers, so all the microservices can run smoothly as a single unified application. A popular container orchestration platform is Kubernetes. It oversees the and controls where containers run, detects and repairs failures, and balances low between microservices. The third pillar is the development process. Cloud native applications are built using a microservices architecture. Different services are developed, deployed, and scale independently of each other. This requires a high-level collaboration between development and operations teams, as well as a significant investment in automation for the development and deployment process. This is where DevOps comes into play. DevOps is a development practice that emphasizes collaboration, communication and automation between development and operations team, to deliver cloud native applications quickly and reliably. A critical component of DevOps is CICD. It enables teams to automate a software development and deployment process, making it faster and more reliable. The continuous integration part of CICD refers to the practice of regularly emerging co-changes into a share repository, and running automated tests to ensure that the code is working as expected. The continuous delivery part of CICD refers to the practice of automating the deployment of the software to production environments, often through the use of automated deployment pipelines. The last pillar is the adoption of cloud native open standards. As a cloud native ecosystem matures, critical components become standardized, and best practices become widely available. Being cloud native means leveraging these standardized components as building blocks, and following these best practices as they become available. Let's walk through some well-known standards and projects behind these standards. We already mentioned Kubernetes. It is a well-known orchestration project. Distributed tracing is an essential component of observability. It tracks requests as they propagate through a maze of microservices. Some well-known projects are Yeager, Zipken, and Open Telemetry. ServiceMash is a core infrastructure layer for managing service to service communication between microservices, Istio and Lincardee are some examples. By leveraging open standards, you freeze developers from having to worry about common functionalities, like logging, tracing, and service discovery. This allows developers to focus on what matters, which is their own microservice applications. So, when should we adopt a cloud native strategy? The answer is, it depends. If the application is more and relatively simple, a cloud native approach may not be necessary. In a traditional monolithic architecture or a simpler deployment model may be sufficient. However, for larger and more complex applications, cloud native can offer a wide range of benefits, such as increased scalability, availability, and faster development cycles. Ultimately, the decision to adopt a cloud native strategy should be based on a careful evaluation of the application's requirements and the organization's resources. When done right, a cloud native approach can help organizations build and deploy applications. They are more reliable, scalable, and resilient in a shorter amount of time. **译文**

什么是云原生？它与云计算有何不同？我们应该采用云原生吗？让我们深入探讨一下。  
“云原生”这一术语似乎最早出现在大约10年前，当时Netflix在2013年的AWS reinvent大会上讨论了其网络级应用架构。那时的术语含义与今天略有不同。但有一点始终未变：当时没有明确的定义，如今依然如此。它对不同的人有着不同的含义。在本视频中，我们将分享我们对“云原生”这一术语的理解，并探讨它为何重要以及在何种情况下重要。首先，云原生带来了哪些承诺？云原生是为在云上构建网络级应用而设计的一种蓝图，这些应用具备更高的可用性和可扩展性。它承诺提升敏捷性，使团队能够快速交付新功能而不牺牲可用性，从而更快地响应不断变化的客户需求。在深入探讨云原生如何实现这些承诺之前，我们先来了解一下它的近亲——云计算。在最基础的形式中，云计算是指在由云提供商管理的计算资源上运行应用程序，而无需我们自己购买和管理硬件。将现有的单体应用从本地环境迁移到云端是一个很好的起点。这使团队能够摆脱对硬件基础设施的管理。这种快速假设的新计算资源可以轻松扩展。然而，仅仅在云端运行一个应用程序，并不能使其成为云原生应用。要认为一个应用程序是云原生的，至少需要考虑四个支柱。第一个支柱是应用架构。云原生应用由多个称为微服务的独立小型服务组成。传统上，开发人员构建的是包含所有所需功能的单一二进制文件的单体应用。快速开发、测试和部署单体应用非常困难。扩展单体应用也具有挑战性。通过使用云原生方法，开发人员将一个大型应用的功能拆分成更小的微服务。这些服务被设计得小巧精悍，使团队能够自主拥有自己的服务，并按照自己的时间表独立地进行部署和扩展。这些微服务之间是松耦合的。它们通过定义明确的 API 相互通信。例如，一个电子商务应用可能由一个购物车服务组成，该服务会与支付服务和库存服务进行交互。第二个支柱是容器和容器编排。云原生应用被打包到容器中。容器是一种轻量级组件，包含了在任何环境中运行微服务所需的一切。容器编排对于大型云原生应用来说是一个关键组成部分。随着微服务数量的增长，容器编排可以管理大量的容器，从而确保所有微服务能够作为一个统一的整体平稳运行。一个流行的容器编排平台是 Kubernetes。它负责监督和控制容器的运行位置，检测和修复故障，并在微服务之间平衡负载。第三个支柱是开发流程。云原生应用采用微服务架构进行构建。不同的服务可以独立地开发、部署和扩展。这需要开发与运维团队之间进行高层级的协作，并在开发和部署流程方面投入大量资源进行自动化。这正是 DevOps 发挥作用的地方。DevOps 是一种强调开发与运维团队之间协作、沟通和自动化的开发实践，旨在快速可靠地交付云原生应用。DevOps 的一个关键组成部分是 CICD。它使团队能够自动化软件开发和部署流程，使其更快、更可靠。CICD 的持续集成部分指的是定期将代码变更合并到共享存储库中，并运行自动化测试以确保代码按预期工作的实践。CICD 的持续交付部分指的是将软件自动部署到生产环境的实践，通常通过自动化部署流水线实现。最后一个支柱是采用云原生开放标准。随着云原生生态系统的成熟，关键组件逐渐实现标准化，最佳实践也变得越来越普及。云原生意味着利用这些标准化组件作为构建模块，并在最佳实践出现时加以遵循。让我们来看看一些广为人知的标准以及其背后的项目。我们已经提到了 Kubernetes，它是一个著名的编排项目。分布式追踪是可观测性的重要组成部分，它用于追踪请求在错综复杂的微服务之间的传播路径。一些知名的项目包括 Yeager、Zipkin 和 OpenTelemetry。ServiceMesh 是管理微服务间通信的核心基础设施层，Istio 和 Linkerd 就是其中的典型代表。通过采用开放标准，开发者就不必再操心日志记录、追踪和服务发现等通用功能，从而能够专注于真正重要的事情——他们自己的微服务应用。那么，我们何时应该采用云原生策略呢？答案是：视情况而定。如果应用较为简单，可能就没有必要采用云原生方法。对于传统的单体架构或更简单的部署模式来说，可能已经足够。然而，对于规模更大、更复杂的应用程序而言，云原生方案可以提供诸多优势，例如更高的可扩展性、可用性以及更快的开发周期。最终，是否采用云原生策略，应基于对应用程序需求与组织资源的仔细评估。只要实施得当，云原生方法能够帮助组织在更短的时间内构建和部署更加可靠、可扩展且具备弹性的应用程序。如果您喜欢我们的视频，可能也会喜欢我们的系统设计时事通讯。我们涵盖大规模系统设计的相关主题与趋势，已拥有 25 万读者订阅。立即订阅：block.bybikeol.com。
